{"type":"agent_message","id":"amsg_01a1640d-cca1-7663-8906-a7b5e9c9cd02","author":"/root/depth_how_architect","recipient":"/root","content":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER\nTask name: /root\nSender: /root/depth_how_architect\nPayload:\n### Overview\n\nI recommend projecting each accepted continuous hit into `gl_FragDepth`, with the composite pass still owning the single eye-to-surface Beer operation. An optical-distance-only MRT is initially less invasive, but cannot correct the existing raster visibility error by itself.\n\nThis is a bounded TEST recommendation. The continuous solver supports known deep water, fixed phase, zero interaction ripples and a restricted footprint. It does not establish general shoreline, stacked-water or secondary-interface visibility.\n\n### Key Concepts\n\n- `worldPosition` is the coarse displaced raster position.\n- `opticalPoint` is the continuous first intersection on the same camera ray.\n- `depthtex1` supplies the immutable pre-translucent opaque visibility reference.\n- `depthtex0` supplies the surviving post-translucent depth used by composite.\n- `distanceToEye` is currently reconstructed from `depthtex0`, not `opticalPoint`.\n- Surface-to-receiver attenuation and eye-to-surface attenuation are separate legs. Moving the surface must not combine them or apply Beer twice.\n\n### How It Works\n\nThe vertex shader displaces water vertices, then projects the resulting mesh. See `shaders/gbuffers_water.vsh:32-45`.\n\nThe fragment shader reconstructs opaque receivers from `depthtex1`, evaluates optical normals, traces transmission and reflection, and writes interface radiance. Its original positions come from the coarse raster mesh. See `shaders/gbuffers_water.fsh:268-345`.\n\nThe hit1 generator injects a continuous first-hit solve and uses that point for normal evaluation and trace origins. It deliberately leaves hardware depth and several lighting/path inputs unchanged. See `tools/water_continuous_candidate.py:19-34,73-76`.\n\nComposite reconstructs its view position from `depthtex0`, computes `min(length(view),far)`, and applies one Beer/in-scattering operation. Consequently, hit1 can shade the continuous surface while attenuating the distance to a different, coarse surface. See `shaders/composite.fsh:21-29,49-52`.\n\n```text\nRaster coverage → continuous hit → interface radiance + corrected hardware depth\nOpaque snapshot ───────────────→ transmission/reflection receiver visibility\nCorrected depthtex0 ────────────→ composite eye distance → one Beer operation\n```\n\n### Where Things Live\n\n- `tools/water_continuous_interface_test.glsl:19-40` owns the bounded solve and explicit failure statuses.\n- `tools/water_continuous_candidate.py:14-38` owns generated TEST modifications.\n- `shaders/gbuffers_water.fsh:276-277,333-345,434-443` contains the remaining surface/path-origin inconsistencies.\n- `shaders/composite.fsh:23-52` owns the eye leg and opaque-only caustic attribution.\n- `shaders/lib/water.glsl:42-46,59-79` defines path proxies, Beer attenuation and scatter.\n- `shaders/deferred.fsh:17-25` creates the opaque color/Sun snapshots paired with `depthtex1`.\n- `shaders/composite1.fsh`, `composite2.fsh` and `final.fsh` consume color, not depth. They need no physical-depth behavior change.\n- `GlReadback.java:37-55,73-80` currently accepts only RGBA32F color samplers. Depth needs a separate strict boundary.\n\n### Gotchas\n\nWriting `gl_FragDepth` statically makes its value undefined on paths that do not assign it. Initialize it to `gl_FragCoord.z` before the nonwater early return at `gbuffers_water.fsh:261-266`, then override only for validated solved hits.\n\nDo not use an `early_fragment_tests` qualifier. The corrected point can be nearer than an opaque occluder even when raster depth was farther. A raster-depth early rejection would lose that valid fragment.\n\nThe solver preserves camera-ray direction, but not raster coverage. Corrected depth cannot create fragments outside the coarse mesh silhouette.\n\nThe exact `depth==opaqueDepth` test in composite is useful. A corrected water depth should remain different from the immutable opaque copy. Do not replace it with a permissive distance tolerance.\n\n### Caller Usage and Shape\n\nProposed generated TEST usage, not implemented:\n\n```glsl\ngl_FragDepth = gl_FragCoord.z;\n\n// Existing eligibility and continuous solve.\nWaterPrimaryHit hit = waterPrimaryHit(\n    worldPosition, opticalPoint, continuousStatus);\n\nvec3 surfacePoint = hit.point;\nvec3 surfaceView = hit.viewPoint;\n\n// Existing optical normal and transport code.\nif (hit.accepted)\n    gl_FragDepth = hit.windowDepth;\n```\n\n```glsl\nstruct WaterPrimaryHit {\n    bool accepted;\n    vec3 point;\n    vec3 viewPoint;\n    float windowDepth;\n    float eyeDistance;\n};\n\nWaterPrimaryHit waterPrimaryHit(\n    vec3 rasterPoint,\n    vec3 solvedPoint,\n    int solveStatus\n) {\n    // not implemented\n}\n```\n\nThe helper should default to the raster point. Accept a solved point only when status is solved, coordinates and clip values are finite, clip W is positive, projected UV agrees with the current pixel within a specified numerical tolerance, and projected depth is in range. Use `gl_DepthRange` when converting clip depth rather than silently assuming its state.\n\nA validation failure should retain the existing raster fallback and an observable rejection status. It must not clamp the solved depth toward an occluder.\n\n### Alternatives and Rationale\n\n**Candidate A. Correct hardware depth. Recommended.**\n\nThe continuous point becomes the authoritative primary surface for accepted fragments. Hardware depth testing resolves it against existing opaque and earlier depth-writing geometry. Later geometry sees its corrected depth. Composite automatically reconstructs the same point without learning the solver.\n\nThis gives the smallest shared public surface. The solver stays in water shading; composite retains its existing distance reconstruction. The cost is requiring explicit depth-state, occlusion and coverage qualification.\n\n**Candidate B. Keep raster depth and publish optical transport.**\n\nWater writes an authoritative point/distance/status MRT. Composite chooses that distance for Beer while leaving hardware depth unchanged.\n\nThis corrects attenuation for already-visible water but cannot repair primary visibility. If the solved point lies behind an opaque ceiling while raster water lies in front, composite still receives the wrong visible surface. Rejecting the record merely restores the inconsistent raster path. Correcting the result would require replacing water color with the opaque snapshot and correctly restoring its Sun attribution, then accounting for later translucency and layer ordering. That expands this unit substantially.\n\nA transport MRT is appropriate for a deliberately depth-independent volumetric representation. It is not an equivalent substitute for this surface correction.\n\n### Origin Changes\n\nFor accepted hits, move these inputs together.\n\n- Initial `straightPath` must use `opaquePosition-surfaceView`. Underwater `waterRefractionPath()` returns that proxy directly.\n- Initial `verticalDepth` must reference the solved absolute surface Y.\n- `shadowVisibility()` at `gbuffers_water.fsh:345` must use the solved surface point.\n- Reflected-source `incidentDepth` at line 442 must use solved absolute surface Y.\n- Existing reflection/refraction origins remain at the solved point.\n- Pass solved surface Y to trace parameters, even where the current underwater branch does not use it.\n\nKeep raster derivatives, raster geometric normals and eligibility unchanged. Do not globally overwrite `absolutePosition`, because it currently also carries raster footprint and geometry responsibilities.\n\nKeep composite’s eye-local scatter illumination at the eye. It should not move to the interface. Keep opaque caustic entry approximations and above-water caustic/foam branches outside this unit. Unknown, shallow, nonwater and failed solves retain the original path.\n\n### Artifact Ownership\n\n- The generator owns exclusive new TEST packs and schema versions.\n- The helper owns accepted primary-hit projection, not receiver visibility.\n- Water owns interface radiance and primary fragment depth.\n- Composite owns the single eye leg.\n- The observer owns raw same-FINAL records and identity/state checks.\n- The parent owns all native/Minecraft execution, exact V2 scope and restoration.\n\nReuse C2 as composite’s actual `(distanceToEye, transmission.rgb)` output. Composite writes it fullscreen, so no stale geometry record survives. Change the observer schema explicitly. Preserve the old geometry evidence and hashes.\n\nDepth sampling must use a new method with separately pinned actual depth formats. Do not broaden the RGBA32F color guard. Keep `depthtex0`, `depthtex1` and required matrices active through preview-only FINAL branches, then capture their actual bindings and values with preview zero.\n\n### Fail-First and Actual Checks\n\nThe minimized native regression should exercise both occlusion directions.\n\n1. Raster water is nearer than an opaque plane, but the solved hit is farther. Opaque must win.\n2. Raster water is farther than the plane, but the solved hit is nearer. Water must win despite its original raster depth.\n3. An unobstructed solved hit must reconstruct the solved eye distance and exactly one Beer operation.\n\nChoose an opaque depth strictly between raster and solved depths. Assert C0 winner, updated `depthtex0`, unchanged `depthtex1`, reconstructed distance and RGB transmission. The old hit1 path must fail these checks. Record actual NVIDIA renderer and depth conversion precision.\n\nFor actual near/deep same-FINAL captures, compare accepted C3 points against captured matrices and raw `depthtex0`, then independently compare C2 distance/transmission against that point. Require unchanged normal/Fresnel results on mutually visible supported pixels.\n\nAdd an isolated TEST opaque-ceiling/occluder case before accepting changed visibility. Include a normal fully blocking ceiling to detect leakage. If a real-world fixture cannot straddle the old/new depth interval, retain the native straddle proof and explicitly leave actual straddle acceptance open.\n\nThese checks do not accept R2, general translucency, dynamic ripples, full radiance, visual completion or performance."}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792679988.3858}}

## 2026-10-22T14:40:03.424Z source line 58571

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a1640e-0760-7180-ad56-f54c2e4ba402","call_id":"call_abea7a63f0394360b6d843424c524aa1","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.1 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"9120ce\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000009107,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":6360,\"output\":\"# Aligning underwater first-hit depth and eye attenuation (hit2): explanation and design\\n\\n## Overview\\nThe hit1 candidate solves the continuous water surface at `opticalPoint`. It then uses that point for the normal and for the reflection/refraction start points. Everything that measures distance to the surface still uses the coarse polygon:\\n- **Hardware depth:** it comes from the rasterized fragment, `gl_FragCoord.z`.\\n- **Composite:** it rebuilds `distanceToEye` from `depthtex0` (`shaders/composite.fsh:23-26`) and applies the eye-to-surface Beer attenuation once (`:49-52`).\\n- **gbuffers_water:** `straightPath` (`shaders/gbuffers_water.fsh:276`) and `shadowVisibility(worldPosition,n)` (`:345`) still use the raster point.\\n\\nThe surface is shaded at one point and attenuated as if it were at another.\\n\\n## Key concepts\\n- **Same ray.** `continuousWaterPoint` searches along the line from the eye through the pixel's raster point (`tools/water_continuous_interface_test.glsl:21`). The solved point projects back to the same pixel center. Only its depth along the ray changes, by up to `heightBound/ray.y`.\\n- **Depth is a transport record.** For an underwater eye, composite's single Beer term is the eye-to-surface leg. The refraction term from gbuffers carries no Beer (`:420`, where `refraction=behind`). Reflection applies its own Beer from its origin (`:449-451`). Once the reflection origin moves, the eye leg has to end at the same point.\\n- **`depthtex1`** is Iris's copy of depth before translucents. It is the opaque reference in both programs and must not change.\\n\\n## How it works now (hit1)\\n`main` computes `viewPos`, `straightPath` and `opaqueDepth` from the raster point. Inside the eligible branch (`underwater && footprint*1.25<=0.035 && column≥0.8`) the solver sets `opticalPoint`. The wave and normal are then evaluated there, `opticalViewPos` feeds both traces, and receivers start from `opticalPoint+n*0.06`. Composite later reads `depthtex0`, which still holds the raster depth.\\n\\n## Where things live\\n- Candidate generator: `tools/water_continuous_candidate.py`. It patches the hit1 zips by anchor with `probe.replace_one` and never touches `shaders/`.\\n- Solver: `tools/water_continuous_interface_test.glsl`.\\n- Readback: `hit1/observer-sources/.../GlReadback.java`. `sampler()` hard-requires `format==34836`, i.e. RGBA32F (`:45`). `rawFloats` rejects non-finite values.\\n- Channel schema: `PairedObserver.java:117,217`.\\n- Native harness: `native-opticalhit1-zoom3/water-paired-native-zoom.cpp`. It currently runs with `GL_DEPTH_TEST` disabled and no depth attachment (`:94-95`).\\n\\n## Gotchas\\n- If any path writes `gl_FragDepth`, every path must write it, including the non-water `return` at `:261-266`. The write also turns off early-Z for every translucent-terrain fragment. Never add `early_fragment_tests`, because it would silently discard the write.\\n- `/* DRAWBUFFERS:02 */` maps `gl_FragData[1]` to colortex2. Use `RENDERTARGETS: 0,2` and say so explicitly.\\n- Writing depth can change which fragments pass the depth test, both against opaque geometry and against later translucents, particles or the hand.\\n- Composite treats `depth==opaqueDepth` as \\\"no water in this pixel\\\". A corrected depth exactly equal to the opaque depth would be misread, so the comparison must be strict.\\n- The depth range cannot be assumed to be [0,1] with plain perspective. Only a native fixture plus the actual captured depth bytes settle that.\\n- No derivatives may be taken of `opticalPoint`, because it comes from a divergent branch.\\n\\n## The two alternatives\\n\\n**A. Write `gl_FragDepth` as the projected `opticalPoint`.**\\n- **Pros:** The depth buffer stays the single authority. Composite is untouched, and every later `depthtex0` reader sees the same first hit. No new data path is needed.\\n- **Cons:** Visibility can change, and early-Z is lost.\\n- **Opaque risk:** The solve bracket ignores opaque geometry. A crest can land behind a block within about one bound of the surface, such as a cave ceiling 0.11 m above the water, a pillar top, kelp or a boat. A raster fragment hidden behind such a block could also become visible.\\n- **Fix:** Accept the solution only if both the raster depth and the projected optical depth are strictly in front of `depthtex1`, and the optical depth is greater than 0. Otherwise fall back to the raster value for everything. With that guard, pass/fail against opaque geometry is unchanged by construction.\\n\\n**B. Raster depth unchanged; composite reads an authoritative optical record.**\\n- **Pros:** No visibility change and no early-Z loss.\\n- **Cons:** It needs a new MRT in the production path of both packs, which is an extra producer write you ruled out. It also needs an ownership key, an exact match on the stored raster `gl_FragCoord.z`, to reject records left stale by later translucents. Every `depthtex0` consumer other than composite stays inconsistent. And the observer would be checking the same channel composite consumes, so it would not be independent.\\n\\n**Recommendation: A with the opaque guard as the candidate's transport.** B's shape is kept only as passive instrumentation: in the paired pack, composite writes what it actually applied into C2.\\n\\n## Caller usage and signatures (sketch, not implemented)\\n```glsl\\n// helper (appended to water_continuous_interface_test.glsl or a hit2 sibling)\\nfloat opticalWindowDepth(vec3 relativePoint);            // gbufferProjection*gbufferModelView, z/w*0.5+0.5\\nbool  opticalDepthAdmitted(float rasterZ,float opticalZ,float opaqueZ); // rasterZ<opaqueZ && opticalZ<opaqueZ && opticalZ>0\\n```\\nChanges to the hit1 water source, each made by `replace_one` with a single anchor:\\n```glsl\\nvoid main() { gl_FragDepth=gl_FragCoord.z;               // every path, including the non-water return/discard\\n...\\nif(continuousStatus==0) {                                 // inside the solve block, BEFORE the wave is evaluated\\n    float z=opticalWindowDepth(solved);\\n    if(opticalDepthAdmitted(gl_FragCoord.z,z,opaqueDepth)) { opticalPoint=solved; opticalDepth=z; }\\n    else continuousStatus=4;                              // 4 = depth/occlusion rejected → everything stays raster\\n}\\n...\\nvec3 opticalViewPos=...;                                  // existing hit1 line\\nif(continuousStatus==0) {\\n    straightPath=opaqueDepth<0.999999?clamp(length(opaquePosition-opticalViewPos),0.0,48.0):48.0;\\n    gl_FragDepth=opticalDepth;\\n}\\nfloat visibility=shadowVisibility(opticalPoint,n)*skyAccess;\\n```\\n- For any status other than 0, `opticalPoint==worldPosition` and the depth write equals `gl_FragCoord.z`, so the fragment is meant to behave exactly as in hit1.\\n- `opticalDepth` is declared next to `opticalPoint`.\\n- In the paired composite only: `/* RENDERTARGETS: 0,2 */`, then `gl_FragData[1]=vec4(distanceToEye, isEyeInWater==1?transmission:vec3(-1.0))`. The values are always finite. C0's math is unchanged.\\n\\n## Which origins move\\n**Moved in this unit:**\\n- Hardware first-hit depth, and through it composite's `distanceToEye`, transmission and scatter weight.\\n- The `straightPath`/`initialPath` origin of the refraction trace.\\n- The shadow-visibility point that feeds `incidentIrradiance` and `scatter`.\\n\\n**Deliberately left outside:**\\n- **Wave inputs:** `footprint`, `rasterFacet`, the filter width, `normalVariance` and the eligibility rule. Moving them would change the waves.\\n- `v`: the direction is the same ray, so only floating-point rounding differs.\\n- `lightmap`/`skyAccess`: interpolated from vertices.\\n- The reflected-receiver `incidentDepth` from `absolutePosition.y` (`:442`): it is a horizontal-surface approximation of the receiver's own sun path, which belongs to the R2/source-radiance work.\\n- `verticalDepth`, foam, `receiverSolarTransmission`, caustics and the trace `surfaceY` arguments: all above-water only.\\n- Ripples: zero in this domain.\\n- Composite's `depth==opaqueDepth` receiver branch and its `aqualumenGrid.w` incident depth.\\n\\n## Artifact ownership\\n| Artifact | Owner | Lifecycle |\\n|---|---|---|\\n| `opticalPoint`, status 4, depth write, origins | hit2 water source in both zips (control and paired), from new `tools/water_depth_candidate.py` pinned to the hit1 zip SHAs | per fragment |\\n| Main depth → `depthtex0` | Iris; the water program owns only its own fragments | per frame |\\n| `depthtex1` | Iris copy before translucents | must stay byte-identical |\\n| C2 (paired only) | composite: (distance, transmission RGB). gbuffers' C2 geometry write may stay, since composite overwrites it, so the water instrumentation is otherwise byte-equal to hit1 | composite → FINAL |\\n| C1, C3, C12–C15 | unchanged hit1 meanings | |\\n| FINAL preview-only branch | paired pack; references `depthtex0`, `depthtex1`, `gbufferProjectionInverse`, `gbufferModelViewInverse` so they stay active | |\\n| Observer 0.1.0-hit2 | new `GlReadback.depthSampler` and `matrix(program,name)` (`glGetUniformfv`, type 35676); colour guard stays strict RGBA32F | read-only, state-preserving |\\n| Analysis | new `.audit/` script, hash pinned; new report directory; no overwrites | |\\n\\n**`depthSampler`:**\\n- Accept only GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT24, GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT32F, GL_DEPTH24_STENCIL8 or GL_DEPTH32F_STENCIL8 (33190, 36012, 35056, 36013), base level 0 and the exact size. Record the actual format. Anything else fails, with no fallback.\\n- Require `TEXTURE_COMPARE_MODE==NONE`.\\n- Read with `glGetTexImage(GL_TEXTURE_2D,0,GL_DEPTH_COMPONENT,GL_FLOAT)` inside `preserving(REAL)`.\\n- Alias checks: `depthtex0`, `depthtex1` and all colour textures must be distinct.\\n\\n## Tests\\n\\n**1. Native fail-first (minimized), extending the zoom3 harness with a real depth path:**\\n- Attach a DEPTH_COMPONENT32F buffer. Pre-fill it with the fixture's opaque depth, with `GL_LEQUAL` and depth mask on.\\n- Draw the zoomed underwater quad. Add a composite-like pass that rebuilds the distance from depth and writes it to RGBA32F. A fixture-only target carries `|opticalPoint−eye|`.\\n- **Validity first:** at least 10⁴ pixels must solve with status 0, or the run is invalid, not passed. This is the hit1 lesson about fixtures where nothing was eligible.\\n- **Expected:** with hit1 shaders, mismatch above the depth-quantization tolerance on more than 0 pixels (this must fail). With hit2, 0 mismatches.\\n- **Tolerance per pixel:** about 2·(d²/near)·ulp(z) + 1e-6·d, with `near` taken from the matrix.\\n- **Sanity:** for status other than 0, `opticalWindowDepth(worldPosition)` must equal `gl_FragCoord.z` within one ulp. This validates the depth formula and range.\\n\\n**2. Native opaque-occluder test.**\\n- **Setup:** using hit1 C3, put an opaque quad in the pre-filled depth in two places. (a) Between the raster and optical depths where the optical point is farther. (b) Between them where the optical point is nearer.\\n- **Expected:** the set of covered pixels equals hit1's exactly, and the guarded pixels report status 4 with raster depth.\\n- **Negative control:** an unguarded variant must fail both cases.\\n- This shows the guard works natively. It does not prove anything about Iris attachments.\\n\\n**3. Actual same-FINAL checks** (Lab UUID b22dbccb-2970-41e6-847f-f183b1ec923d only; frozen 17 s, ripples 0, the existing near and deep poses):\\n- **New baseline:** run a \\\"hit1d\\\" pack, which is hit1 shading plus the hit2 observer, composite C2 and depth readback. This is new data, not a rerun, because hit1 never captured depth. It must show nonzero depth/distance mismatches on status-0 pixels (the in-game fail-first).\\n- **Depth matches optical point:** for status-0 pixels in hit2, `depthtex0` equals the projection of C3 using the captured matrices within the tolerance above, for the format actually read.\\n- **Distance chain:** C2.x equals |C3.xyz − eye|, and for other statuses |C1.xyz − eye|, where eye = `gbufferModelViewInverse`·0. C2.x also matches the rebuild from `depthtex0`.\\n- **Transmission:** C2.yzw equals `waterTransmission(C2.x)` within float ulps.\\n- **Unchanged against hit1d:** `depthtex1` is byte-identical. `depthtex0` is byte-identical outside status 0. C1 is byte-identical.\\n- **Counts:** report the number of status-4 pixels and the full distribution of depth shifts.\\n- **Passivity:** paired C0 equals the unobserved hit2 control C0 for every case.\\n- **In-game occluder:** if no opaque geometry falls inside the bound band in the Lab frames, the in-game occluder result is `null` (not exercised), not passed. A dedicated Lab-only scene variant is your call.\\n\\n**Not claimed:**\\n- The shadow-point move has no in-game channel. It is checked only by source diff and the native fixture, so it stays open evidence.\\n- Early-Z/performance cost: frame times are recorded, but no gate is passed.\\n- Other translucents, particles and hand interactions with the corrected depth: unknown/shallow/contact/cave worlds, R2, physical, visual and release gates all stay open.\\n- Ordinary saves and configs stay untouched. After the run, restore the normal instance to 0.6/physics 0.2 and stop the client.\\n\\nI read 16 files and used no subagents or commands. Plan mode asked for a plan file, but I had no write tool, so this message is the whole handoff. The paired pack's `shaders.properties` is inside the zip and I didn't read it. Before trusting C3, C12 or C14, check that it turns blending off for those buffers, since their alpha channels hold data.\\nContinue Aqualumen with Codex and actual Claude collaboration. READ ONLY source analysis/design. No GPU, game, network, commands, subagents, or canonical edits. Return a focused how-style explanation then a design sketch/rationale, <=1700 words, <=20 targeted file reads. Parent owns all runtime work. No copied shader-pack sources.\\n\\nUser authorized continued implementation with unrestricted Codex/Claude consumption. Goal is consistent underwater water-surface hit depth and attenuation before later R2/general visibility work. Do not settle for another plan-only handoff. Design must identify the smallest evidence-backed implementation and fail-first check.\\n\\nGrounding sources under /home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen:\\n- AGENTS.md.\\n- reports/2026-10-22-underwater-rim-fix/SUMMARY.md and REVIEW-ADDENDUM.json.\\n- tools/water_continuous_candidate.py and tools/water_continuous_interface_test.glsl.\\n- shaders/gbuffers_water.fsh, shaders/composite.fsh, shaders/lib/water.glsl and shaders/lib/uniforms.glsl.\\n- tools/paired-water-probe/dev/aqualumen/paired/GlReadback.java, plus existing generated hit1 observer if needed in reports/2026-10-22-underwater-rim-fix/hit1/observer-sources.\\n\\nEstablished actual evidence, do not rerun old tests: hit1 frozen17s/ripples0 optical correction has same raster position/view/geometry bytes as optics3. Actual near/deep TIR versus a separate continuous-intersection implementation of SAME wave field has0 mismatch on1,436,459/1,437,695 supported pixels. Normal differences<=9.1e-5. Root excludes156/24pixels at10micrometer; critical1e-3 excludes3385/2281. Actual circles/wave folds remain and are not all bugs. All previous evidence and source hashes preserved. Normal0.6/physics0.2 restored and client stopped.\\n\\nCurrent inconsistency: hit1 computes n and refraction/reflection origins at opticalPoint, but hardware depth and composite distanceToEye still come from coarse raster depthtex0. Shadow visibility uses worldPosition, source incidentDepth uses absolutePosition.y, initial straight

Use for poteto, /poteto-mode, or requests to work in this style.\\n---\\n\\n# Poteto mode\\n\\nBundled skills live at `../<name>/SKILL.md` from this file, so put this file's absolute path in every `poteto-agent` prompt.\\n\\n## Platform Adaptation\\n\\nThese skills use Claude Code tool names (the `Skill` tool, the `Agent` tool, `AskUserQuestion`) and the Claude model names the `Agent` tool accepts, as each Models section lists them. On Claude Code they work as written. On Codex, read [`references/codex-tools.md`](references/codex-tools.md) for the Codex equivalent of a Claude tool, model, or skill named by these workflows. On Pi, read [`references/pi-tools.md`](references/pi-tools.md) instead. On GitHub Copilot, CLI or app, read [`references/copilot-tools.md`](references/copilot-tools.md) instead. On Muse, read [`references/muse-tools.md`](references/muse-tools.md) instead. Other runtimes can discover the same Agent Skills tree, but they must use their own tool, model, and configuration equivalents. No one of these mappings is a cross-runtime map.\\n\\nUse the session's task-tracking tools for the todolist. On Claude Code these are `TaskCreate` and `TaskUpdate`, or `TodoWrite` when configured. Availability depends on the model; `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` enables them through the environment or a project's `.claude/settings.local.json` `env` block. See the [task-tool documentation](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference#task-tool-availability). If no task-tracking tool is available, keep an uncommitted `todo.md` Markdown checklist in the work dir, next to the decision trail, with the playbook steps verbatim and each `skip: <reason>` line. When several sessions share the checkout, name it `.audit/<task-slug>.todo.md`, beside that session's `.audit/<task-slug>.tsv` trail, so they never write one file.\\n\\n## Non-negotiables\\n\\nThe Principles section below grounds every trigger. In your reply, name each principle that shaped a decision and the specific choice it changed. Cite only principles whose leaf SKILL.md you read this session.\\n\\nRemaining triggers:\\n\\n- Nontrivial change, architecture decision, or \\\"are we sure?\\\" → the **how** skill.\\n- About to `AskUserQuestion` on a \\\"which approach\\\", \\\"how should I\\\", or \\\"what should this do\\\" fork → classify it before you ask. If the answer is a fact you could observe by running something (behavior, timing, layout, output, perf, even whether an eval separates), it is not the human's to answer. Sketch it via the Prototype playbook (`playbooks/prototype.md`) and let the result decide. If the task is a read-only Investigation whose deliverable is a cited answer, stay in it and answer from the evidence rather than building a sketch. Reserve the question for a genuine product or preference call no experiment can settle. Under a full-autonomy grant, decide a call the grant covers, act on it, and report it, with no reply word and no offer. Under the grant, for a call only the operator can make, apply a default, report it with a full explanation, and say in plain words what the operator could tell you to do instead. The operator answers in their own words. Never give a shorthand token to type back. Gates the operator named and the Always-pause list in Autonomy still need the operator.\\n- Any code → name the data shape first, and choose its organizing structure per **principle-model-the-domain**.\\n- Code crossing a function boundary → the **architect** skill, parallel design exploration before implementing.\\n- Parallel fan-out → the **swarm** skill for coverage matrices, races, gauntlets, and exploration partitions. Use **arena** for design or code bakeoffs with base selection and grafting.\\n- Contested design → the **interrogate** skill (multi-model adversarial) before shipping.\\n- Nontrivial multi-step → write the throughput checkpoint (Feature step 3).\\n- Any prose surface, your reply to the user included → the **unslop** skill. Write the reply per **Writing the reply**. Agent-facing prose also follows the **plugin-dev:skill-development** skill (Claude Code's authoring guidance for SKILL.md files).\\n- Docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages → the **technical-writing** skill (`/technical-writing`), on top of **unslop**.\\n- Before commit → the **deslop** skill (`/deslop`).\\n- Before review → the **no-comments** skill (`/no-comments`).\\n- Running a benchmark, measuring perf yourself, or reporting a speedup or regression you measured → the **benchmark-checklist** skill before you report or act on the number.\\n- Shipping UI / IDE / CLI → the driver skill. `run` ships with Claude Code and the model can invoke it for CLIs, TUIs, servers, and browser or Electron apps. The bundled `verify` runs only when the user types `/verify`, so for UIs use the project `verify` skill at `.claude/skills/verify/` (generate it with `/create-verification-skill`) and fall back to `run` when the repo has none. For bug fixes, reproduce first on the same surface yourself. Hand to the user only under the narrow Bug fix step 1 exception.\\n- Any PR-status request (\\\"babysit this\\\", \\\"get it green\\\", \\\"address the review-bot comments\\\", and most often \\\"check on PR X\\\" or \\\"anything outstanding on X\\\") → the **Babysit** playbook (`playbooks/babysit.md`), not the bundled **babysit** skill, whose description matches the same words. Never triggered by merely opening a PR. Declare its mode before polling. The playbook's step 1 owns the request-to-mode mapping. Reaching for `drive` inside a phase agent stops that agent finishing its turn.\\n- Asked to land or ship a green stack → the **Shipping** playbook (`playbooks/shipping.md`). Green is not safe. Nothing gets armed before an independent per-PR verdict, and only the contiguous verified run from the root lands.\\n- An automated PR-review bot or the agentic security review commented → skeptical posture. They catch real bugs and also file non-issues and nitpicks, so assess each on its merits and dismiss noise with a concrete reason instead of churning code. Triage fix / dismiss / ask per `references/bugbot-triage.md`.\\n- Deploying to a managed platform (Railway, Fly, Vercel, Heroku, and the like) → load that platform's skill, project-local or installed, before running its CLI, the same way Shipping step 1 resolves the forge before the first PR operation.\\n- A defect found mid-task → severity decides its artifact, not where it turned up. A correctness or data gap gets a tracked issue even when it surfaces while writing a closure doc; a cosmetic margin can stay in the doc.\\n- Broken skill mid-task → fix it in its own PR. Don't block. Don't silently work around it.\\n- Long, autonomous, or multi-phase work, or any task the user steps away from to review later (\\\"going to bed\\\", \\\"trust it when i'm back\\\", \\\"/loop until X\\\") → a decision trail via the **show-me-your-work** skill. Commit it when stakes need an auditable record. Keep it local otherwise.\\n\\n## Principles\\n\\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies. Each leaf is the **principle-<title>** skill, with the title in kebab case, hyphens kept and other punctuation dropped, unless the entry gives its name.\\n\\n**Core**\\n\\n- **Laziness Protocol.** Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\\n- **Foundational Thinking.** Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\\n- **Redesign from First Principles.** Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\\n- **Attack the Premise.** Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it.\\n- **Subtract Before You Add.** Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\\n- **Minimize Reader Load.** Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution.** Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\\n- **Experience First.** Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\\n- **Exhaust the Design Space.** A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\\n- **Build the Lever.** Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\\n\\n**Architecture**\\n\\n- **Model the Domain.** Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\\n- **Boundary Discipline.** Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\\n- **Type System Discipline.** Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\\n- **Make Operations Idempotent.** Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs.** Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State.** Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\\n\\n**Verification**\\n\\n- **Prove It Works.** After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \\\"it compiles\\\".\\n- **Fix Root Causes.** Debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate.\\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation.** Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Assert what the code's users can observe, so the test fails when that behavior breaks and keeps passing when only the internals change.\\n- **Explain the Number.** Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\\n\\n**Delegation**\\n\\n- **Guard the Context Window.** Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\\n- **Never Block on the Human.** Tempted to ask \\\"should I do X?\\\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\\n\\n**Meta**\\n\\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure.** You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\\n\\n## Autonomy\\n\\n**Just do it.** Use any MCP tool. Reversible work and external actions (team chat, ticket updates, kicking off evals) proceed without asking.\\n\\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\\n\\n**Session overrides:** \\\"Don't stop\\\" / \\\"going to bed\\\" / \\\"run until done\\\" / \\\"be fully autonomous\\\" → keep going.\\n\\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \\\"this doesn't earn its place\\\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\\n\\n## Subagents\\n\\n**Use `subagent_type: \\\"pstack:poteto-agent\\\"` for any subagent you spawn inside a playbook step** (code-writing delegates, ad-hoc helpers). Plugin agents register under the plugin namespace. The bare name `poteto-agent` errors. The chat that receives `/poteto-mode` runs the playbook. Never spawn a `poteto-agent` to run the playbook itself. Routed workflow skills (`how`, `why`, `interrogate`, `reflect`, `swarm`) set their own `subagent_type` for diverse-model review. Respect what the skill prescribes, don't override to `poteto-agent`.\\n\\n**Defaults for every `Agent` call.** `run_in_background: true`, full tool access (do not pick a subagent_type that strips MCP), file pointers not inlined context, explicit model per role (configurable via `/setup-pstack`. Role defaults in [Models](#models), with \\\"judgment and prose\\\" covering prose and judgment). Code delegates tier by difficulty. The hardest changes (cross-cutting design, gnarly concurrency, subtle algorithms) go to your strongest-judgment model (default in [Models](#models)), whether the task needs judgment on vague intent or is a precisely specified sequence of steps to execute to the letter. Trivial mechanical edits go to your fast code model, the worker model. Every other code change uses its playbook's role line, and prose and judgment use the single-role default. Multi-model panels run the configured panel for diversity, with defaults enumerated in each panel skill's Models section (`arena`, `architect`, `interrogate`). Per-role `/setup-pstack` lines override these defaults and the model choices in the routed skills (`how`, `why`, `arena`, `swarm`, `architect`, `interrogate`, `reflect`). A role with no line keeps its default, and a role line of `inherit-parent` or `auto` runs that role on the parent session's model (omit `model` on the `Agent` call).\\n\\n**Match the model to the job.** Before each spawn, start from the role's line or default and pick the reasoning level the job needs, set as [Reasoning effort](#reasoning-effort) describes. The role's `@level`, or the sheet's `default effort` line, sets its starting level, and `session` starts from the session's level. Raise it one step, up to max, for the hardest or subtlest work. Lower it one step for judgment and two or more steps for routine work. Claude Code sets fast mode for the whole session, so a spawn changes only its effort. Verification is the exception and stays on the `swarm workers` model and effort (default in the **swarm** skill's Models section). Change only the effort level, and keep the model the role names, so a diverse panel stays diverse. If the model does not take that level, use its nearest level. A role on `auto` or `inherit-parent` stays on the parent model. If the `Agent` tool rejects the adjusted level, do not try another level. Use the role's line or default and say so.\\n\\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a1640f-1ea7-7b43-bf04-648bd9ad814b","call_id":"call_fd78e6934ffb42a187a08b5d9add1289","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 1.2 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"3e6ed0\",\"wall_time_seconds\":1.000953317,\"session_id\":8086,\"original_token_count\":11,\"output\":\"Actual Claude crossjudge started read-only\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"5c751b\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000011943,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":2251,\"output\":\"---\\nname: arena\\ndescription: \\\"Spawn N parallel candidates at the same task, pick a base, graft the strongest parts of the losers into it. Use for /arena, 'arena this', 'throw it in the arena', or when one attempt at a non-trivial artifact would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\n---\\n\\n# Arena\\n\\nOn Codex, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOn GitHub Copilot, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/copilot-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOn Muse, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/muse-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOpen a todolist with one entry per phase (Frame, Fan out, Cross-judge, Pick, Graft, Verify) before launching anything.\\n\\n## Phase A: Frame\\n\\nEvery candidate gets the same prompt, so the prompt is the contract.\\n\\n1. State the artifact each candidate is producing.\\n2. Derive the rubric, 3-6 concrete gradeable criteria for what success looks like on *this* task. It is the picker's tool in Phase D. Candidates only see the task.\\n3. Pick the runners. Use the `arena runners` line in `pstack-models.md`. If the sheet or that line is missing, run one each on the defaults in [Models](#models). An `auto` or `inherit-parent` entry in this line or the cross-judge line means the parent model, so omit `model` for it. If the `Agent` tool rejects a configured entry, run that seat on its family's default and say so. Families go by model name, such as Opus, Fable, or Sonnet. With no family match, use the single-role default in [Models](#models). If it rejects a default, use the closest valid slug of the same family from its error message. Spawn more runners when the arena covers multiple design directions. Use the same model N times when the work is generation-bound rather than judgment-sensitive. Pick each subagent's reasoning level for the job, per the Subagents section of the **poteto-mode** skill.\\n4. Give each candidate its own output location (a git worktree where possible, otherwise `/tmp/arena-<slug>/candidate-<n>/`), per the **separate-before-serializing-shared-state** principle skill.\\n\\n## Phase B: Fan out\\n\\nSpawn all N subagents in one message with `run_in_background: true`, each with the task, the path to the shared grounding, its own output path, and instructions to produce the artifact and a short rationale naming the alternatives it considered and what it rejected.\\n\\nIf a candidate produces no output, proceed with N-1 and note the dropout.\\n\\n## Phase C: Cross-judge\\n\\nAfter all Phase B candidates complete, choose one model from the `arena cross-judge pool` line in `pstack-models.md`. If the sheet or that line is missing, choose from the runner defaults in [Models](#models). Prefer a different model family from the parent's. Spawn one readonly judge subagent on that model. It sees the rubric and the candidates by path label, scores each criterion, and recommends a base with rationale. It runs in parallel with your reading in Phase D. Don't spawn it while candidates are still writing.\\n\\n## Phase D: Pick a base\\n\\nRead every candidate end to end before picking. Score each against the rubric criterion by criterion, not on holistic feel, and compare with the cross-judge. Agreement on the base confirms the pick. Disagreement means one of you is biased or the rubric was ambiguous. Read both rationales before deciding.\\n\\nPick the base a future maintainer can extend most easily without breaking invariants. When two feel tied, prefer the cleaner boundary or smaller API, per the Laziness Protocol.\\n\\n## Phase E: Graft\\n\\nWalk each losing candidate once more for what is worth porting into the base, usually one or two things per candidate, not most of it. Fold each graft in by hand, per the **redesign-from-first-principles** principle skill, so the result stays coherent under one mental model. Don't paste mechanically.\\n\\nIf the candidates converge on one shape, that is strong agreement. Note it and ship the consensus shape. No graft is needed. If they wildly diverge, Phase A was under-specified. Reframe and re-run rather than averaging the divergence.\\n\\n## Phase F: Verify\\n\\nVerify the synthesized artifact like any other output, per the **prove-it-works** principle skill. If verification finds a problem the arena missed, either Phase A was wrong (re-frame and re-run) or a candidate caught it and you missed the graft (go back to Phase E). Don't paper over it.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nOne synthesized artifact, with a short synthesis note alongside naming the base and why, the cross-judge's verdict, each graft with its source candidate, what was rejected and why, any convergence or dropouts, and the verification result.\\n\\n## Models\\n\\nRole defaults, stamped from `plugins/pstack/models.json` (edit there, rerun `tools/generate.mjs`). A matching role line in the `pstack-models.md` override sheet overrides each at runtime; `/setup-pstack` writes it and lists its path per runtime.\\n\\n- arena runners: `opus`, `fable`, `sonnet`, `haiku`\\n- arena cross-judge pool: `opus`, `fable`, `sonnet`, `haiku`\\n\\n## Reasoning effort\\n\\nA role value in the override sheet may name a reasoning effort after its model, as in `opus @xhigh`. Levels on Claude Code: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. Which ones apply depends on the model. A value without `@` takes the sheet's `default effort` line, a level or `session`, and `session` when the sheet has no such line. `session` sets no effort, so the dispatch is the usual one. Strip the suffix before reading the model: `inherit-parent` or `auto` still omits `model` at every level, and a model name is passed as `model`. Pass the level as the dispatch's effort field and keep the `subagent_type` you would otherwise use. That is `effort` on the Claude Code `Agent` call and `reasoning_effort` on Codex's `spawn_agent`. waveMode(i,direction,k,weight,phase);\\n        variance+=0.5*weight*weight;\\n        float amplitude=abs(weight)*exp(-0.25*pow(k*0.035,2.0));\\n        heightBound+=amplitude;slopeBound+=amplitude*k;\\n    }\\n    return vec2(heightBound,slopeBound)*(0.5*abs(WAVE_HEIGHT)/sqrt(max(variance,1e-6)));\\n}\\nbool continuousDeepWave(vec3 point,float meanY,out vec3 wave,out vec3 column) {\\n    wave=vec3(0.0);column=vec3(0.0);\\n    if(!localWaterColumn(point.xz,meanY,column) || column.x<0.8)return false;\\n    float variance;\\n    wave=localWaveState(point.xz+cameraPosition.xz,frameTimeCounter,0.035,column,1.0,variance);\\n    return true;\\n}\\nint continuousWaterPoint(vec3 rasterPoint,float meanY,out vec3 point,out vec3 column) {\\n    point=rasterPoint;column=vec3(0.0);\\n    vec3 eye=relativeWorld(vec3(0.0)),ray=normalize(rasterPoint-eye);\\n    vec2 bound=continuousWaterBounds();\\n    if(ray.y<=0.0 || ray.y-bound.y*length(ray.xz)<=0.00001)return 1;\\n    float lo=max(0.0,(meanY-bound.x-eye.y)/ray.y),hi=(meanY+bound.x-eye.y)/ray.y;\\n    if(hi<lo)return 3;\\n    vec3 wave,probeColumn;\\n    if(!continuousDeepWave(eye+lo*ray,meanY,wave,probeColumn))return 2;\\n    if(eye.y+lo*ray.y-meanY-wave.x>0.0)return 3;\\n    if(!continuousDeepWave(eye+hi*ray,meanY,wave,probeColumn))return 2;\\n    if(eye.y+hi*ray.y-meanY-wave.x<0.0)return 3;\\n    for(int step=0;step<24;step++) {\\n        float mid=lo+(hi-lo)*0.5;\\n        vec3 probePoint=eye+mid*ray;\\n        if(!continuousDeepWave(probePoint,meanY,wave,probeColumn))return 2;\\n        if(probePoint.y-meanY-wave.x>0.0)hi=mid;else lo=mid;\\n    }\\n    vec3 solved=eye+(lo+(hi-lo)*0.5)*ray;\\n    if(!continuousDeepWave(solved,meanY,wave,probeColumn))return 2;\\n    point=solved;column=probeColumn;\\n    return 0;\\n}\\n---\\nname: architect\\ndescription: \\\"Sketch types, signatures, and module structure before code, then stay in the loop while implementation fills in. Use for /architect, 'architect this', 'design this', or non-trivial work where jumping to code would lock in the wrong shape.\\\"\\n---\\n\\n# Architect\\n\\nOn Codex, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOn GitHub Copilot, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/copilot-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOn Muse, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/muse-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nSketch types, function signatures, class shapes, and module boundaries with `not implemented` bodies and pseudocode. Synthesize across multiple model perspectives, then fill in code against the chosen sketch.\\n\\n## Start\\n\\nOpen a todolist with one entry per phase before starting.\\n\\n1. Ground\\n2. Sketch\\n3. Agree\\n4. Implement\\n5. Scrap\\n\\n## Phase A: Ground the problem\\n\\nBuild a real mental model of every system the new code touches. Run the **how** skill over the relevant subsystems. Naming a file isn't grounding. Produce the architectural explanation `how` prescribes. If the design redefines ownership or layering, also run the **why** skill on the existing shape so the rationale becomes a constraint, not a guess.\\n\\nSkip Phase A only when the work is genuinely greenfield with no surrounding system to integrate.\\n\\n## Phase B: Sketch\\n\\nRun the **arena** skill with the design-sketch task and the Phase A grounding artifacts. Pass `references/runner-prompt.md` as each runner's prompt.\\n\\nTake the runners from the `architect runners` line in `pstack-models.md`, in place of the `arena runners` line. If the sheet or that line is missing, use the defaults in [Models](#models). Alias and rejected entries follow the runner rules in the **arena** skill's Phase A.\\n\\nDesign it twice. Require at least two structurally distinct candidates before synthesis, even when the first looks sufficient. This is the **exhaust-the-design-space** principle skill made concrete. Whole-shape alternatives, not point fixes inside one shape.\\n\\nScreen every candidate against [`references/design-red-flags.md`](references/design-red-flags.md) before synthesis. Assume the next contributor is an agent that sees only the files it opened, copies the nearest example, and takes the shortest path that compiles. Prefer the design where a change that looks right from one file is right for the whole repo.\\n\\nCompare viable candidates on interface depth. Prefer the design that hides more complexity behind a smaller, simpler public surface. A rich interface can keep call chains short by concentrating capability instead of scattering it across layers.\\n\\nArena returns one synthesized design package. Its synthesis decision fills the rationale's \\\"Synthesis decision\\\" section.\\n\\n## Phase C: Agree (opt-in)\\n\\nDefault: proceed directly to implementation with the synthesized design. No human checkpoint.\\n\\nOpt in to a checkpoint when the invoker explicitly asks: \\\"/architect with checkpoint,\\\" \\\"stop and show me before implementing,\\\" or similar. Then surface the synthesized design and pause for sign-off.\\n\\nThe synthesis can ship as its own commit either way, as the \\\"scaffold first\\\" mode of the **foundational-thinking** principle skill. Planned and scoped breakage during fill-in is fine, per the **outcome-oriented-execution** principle skill. For adversarial pressure on the design before implementing, run the **interrogate** skill on the synthesized sketch.\\n\\nIf the human pushes back on the shape (in a checkpoint or after the fact), treat that as Phase A evidence. Re-ground and re-run Phase B before writing more code.\\n\\n## Phase D: Implement against the sketch\\n\\nReplace `not implemented` bodies with code, pseudocode with logic. The synthesized sketch is the contract. Deviations from it are signal to surface, not friction to absorb silently. If a function needs a parameter the sketch didn't anticipate, ask whether the sketch was wrong, the requirement was missed, or the implementation is overreaching.\\n\\nBefore closing each implementation unit or handing its contract to the next worker, compare accepted deviations with the saved behavior contracts, interfaces, and responsibility assignments. Once the appropriate owner or review process accepts a change, update the affected parts of the existing sketch and rationale. Record the acceptance source there. A change from raising an error to returning a refusal must update the outcome contract before the next unit begins.\\n\\nFor a local deviation that leaves the shared contract intact, record the decision and why the design still holds; a local variable rename need not rewrite the architecture. Keep unaccepted changes and unresolved disagreements visible. Do not call the unit reconciled while the next worker would receive contradictory instructions, or edit the specification merely to justify what was implemented. Use the existing design artifact rather than a parallel record per unit. Repeated structural deviations still trigger Phase E.\\n\\n## Phase E: Scrap when the architecture is wrong\\n\\nIf implementation keeps producing friction the sketch can't absorb, throw the sketch out. Don't bolt fixes onto a wrong design, per the **redesign-from-first-principles** and **fix-root-causes** principle skills.\\n\\nThe signal is a *pattern*, not single instances. Tells:\\n\\n- The same shape of workaround appearing repeatedly across unrelated code.\\n- Multiple unrelated edge cases that all need special-case branches.\\n- Types that need escape hatches (`any`, casts, optional fields always set in practice) to compile.\\n- The \\\"we need a lock\\\" reflex when the sketch said the state wasn't shared.\\n- Callers having to know the abstraction's internal rules to use it.\\n- Two or more independent Phase D deviations of the same shape across the implementation.\\n\\nUse judgment. A few edge cases don't condemn an architecture. Some problems are legitimately complex. Complexity in the data is not complexity in the design.\\n\\nWhen you scrap:\\n\\n1. Re-run the **how** skill over what's been built.\\n2. Redesign as if the new constraints had been day-one assumptions, per redesign-from-first-principles.\\n3. Subtract before adding, per the **subtract-before-you-add** principle skill. The new sketch should be smaller than the old one before it grows.\\n4. Return to Phase B and re-run arena.\\n\\n## Outputs\\n\\nThe caller's usage is written first and the type sketch derived from it. One file with new types and signatures for small changes. Module map plus type definitions for larger work. The rationale ships alongside, shaped per `references/rationale-template.md`, including the usage sketch and the synthesis decision.\\n\\n## Models\\n\\nRole defaults, stamped from `plugins/pstack/models.json` (edit there, rerun `tools/generate.mjs`). A matching role line in the `pstack-models.md` override sheet overrides each at runtime; `/setup-pstack` writes it and lists its path per runtime.\\n\\n- architect runners: `opus`, `fable`, `sonnet`, `haiku`\\n\\n## Reasoning effort\\n\\nA role value in the override sheet may name a reasoning effort after its model, as in `opus @xhigh`. Levels on Claude Code: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. Which ones apply depends on the model. A value without `@` takes the sheet's `default effort` line, a level or `session`, and `session` when the sheet has no such line. `session` sets no effort, so the dispatch is the usual one. Strip the suffix before reading the model: `inherit-parent` or `auto` still omits `model` at every level, and a model name is passed as `model`. Pass the level as the dispatch's effort field and keep the `subagent_type` you would otherwise use. That is `effort` on the Claude Code `Agent` call and `reasoning_effort` on Codex's `spawn_agent`. On Muse it is `effort` on a Workflow child; a `subagent_spawn` dispatch keeps the session's effort.\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"1272b4\",\"wall_time_seconds\":5.001874262,\"session_id\":8086,\"original_token_count\":0,\"output\":\"\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792680115.0910823}}

{"type":"custom_tool_call","id":"ctc_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada20cb704487d09f5fa5c7c0ee5b79","status":"completed","call_id":"call_2bc0b0eb0eb14751b17a76561b8826a6","name":"exec","input":"text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy mktemp -d /tmp/aqualumen-depth-writer.XXXXXX\",\"max_output_tokens\":1000}));\ntext(await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy bash -c 'sed -n \\\"1,210p\\\" /home/kusutohu/.codex/plugins/cache/pstack-claude/pstack/0.9.101/skills/poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md; sed -n \\\"1,180p\\\" /home/kusutohu/.codex/plugins/cache/pstack-claude/pstack/0.9.101/skills/principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state/SKILL.md; sed -n \\\"1,150p\\\" /home/kusutohu/.codex/plugins/cache/pstack-claude/pstack/0.9.101/skills/principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md'\",\"max_output_tokens\":8000}));\n","internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792680139.4418323}}

## 2026-10-22T14:42:24.581Z source line 58610

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16410-2ec5-7fc1-b2d9-c06ca725158f","call_id":"call_2bc0b0eb0eb14751b17a76561b8826a6","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.1 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"1c6180\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000007915,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":9,\"output\":\"/tmp/aqualumen-depth-writer.lIYL1g\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"0695e2\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000007745,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":3356,\"output\":\"# Codex tool mapping for pstack\\n\\npstack skills are written in Claude Code tool language (the `Skill` tool, the `Agent` tool, `AskUserQuestion`, Claude model names). On Codex the skills are the same files; only the tool names resolve differently. Read this when a pstack skill names a Claude tool, a driver or bundled skill, or a Claude model. This file is Codex-specific. Gemini CLI, opencode, Prime Agent, and other runtimes must use their own concrete tools, model names, and configuration paths.\\n\\n## Tool actions\\n\\n| pstack / Claude action | Codex equivalent |\\n|------------------------|------------------|\\n| Read a file | `shell` (`cat`, `head`, `tail`) |\\n| Create / edit / delete a file | `apply_patch` |\\n| Run a shell command | `shell` |\\n| Search file contents / find files | `shell` (`rg`, `grep`, `find`, `ls`) |\\n| Fetch a URL | `shell` with `curl` / `wget` |\\n| Search the web | `web_search` |\\n| Invoke a skill (the `Skill` tool, `/command`) | Skills load natively. Follow the instructions presented. |\\n| Dispatch a subagent (the `Agent`/`Task` tool) | `spawn_agent` |\\n| Dispatch N parallel subagents in one turn | N `spawn_agent` calls in one response |\\n| Wait for a subagent result | `wait_agent` |\\n| Free a finished subagent slot | `close_agent`, when the session exposes it. Some Codex hosts don't. |\\n| Track tasks (the todolist; `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate`, or `TodoWrite` on Claude Code) | `update_plan` |\\n| Ask the human a fixed-choice question (`AskUserQuestion`) | Ask in plain text and let the user answer. Codex has no structured-choice tool. |\\n\\nSubagent dispatch needs `multi_agent` enabled. Add to `~/.codex/config.toml`:\\n\\n```toml\\n[features]\\nmulti_agent = true\\n```\\n\\nWithout it, `spawn_agent` is unavailable and the fan-out skills (`interrogate`, `why`, `how`, `arena`, `reflect`) degrade to a single sequential pass. A required independent review does not degrade. It stays blocked, as poteto-mode's [Subagents](../SKILL.md#subagents) section says.\\n\\nEnabling `multi_agent` does not lift the host's thread limit. A thread-limit error from `spawn_agent` is a capacity error, not a rejected model slug, so the skills' fallback to another model does not apply. After the first capacity rejection, stop spawning. Close a finished agent with `close_agent` before you try again, and where the session has no `close_agent`, do not assume a finished agent's slot is free.\\n\\n## Subagent policy\\n\\npoteto-mode's Subagents section sets Claude-specific defaults (`subagent_type: \\\"pstack:poteto-agent\\\"`, `run_in_background: true`). On Codex:\\n\\n- There is no `poteto-agent` subagent type. Route an ad-hoc subagent through poteto-mode's style by dispatching a `spawn_agent` whose instructions tell it to read the `poteto-mode` skill in full first.\\n- `spawn_agent` calls already run concurrently with your turn, so `run_in_background: true` has no separate flag. Issue the dispatch and continue.\\n- When a role value carries `@<level>`, or the `default effort` line names a level, pass that level as `spawn_agent`'s `reasoning_effort` and keep the dispatch otherwise unchanged. `session` passes no `reasoning_effort`.\\n- There is no `comment-sicko` subagent type either. The **no-comments** skill spawns it on Claude Code; on Codex dispatch a `spawn_agent` whose instructions tell it to read `poteto-mode/references/agents/comment-sicko.md` in full first.\\n- There are no `thermo-nuclear-review-subagent` or `thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review-subagent` types. The **thermos** skill spawns both on Claude Code; on Codex dispatch two `spawn_agent` calls whose instructions tell each to read the `thermo-nuclear-review` or `thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review` skill in full first and review only the diff and file contents in its prompt.\\n- Claude Code runs every subagent on this machine, so the **swarm** skill's workers and the fan-out playbooks (`orchestrate`, `autopilot-full`, `autopilot-stack`) isolate writers with worktrees. The same holds on Codex.\\n- Keep the rest of the policy unchanged. Pass file pointers not inlined context, give each worker its own worktree or branch when they write, review every subagent's diff yourself.\\n\\n## Model names\\n\\nSkills name Claude defaults (a single-role default for code/prose/judgment, a worker model for feature and refactoring code, plus a diverse-model panel for diverse-model panels; each model-consuming skill lists its own in a Models section). These slugs do not resolve on Codex. Substitute your configured Codex models:\\n\\n- Single-model roles: your primary Codex model (for example `gpt-6-sol`).\\n- Roles that default to the Claude worker model (`feature, refactoring`): your fast Codex code model (for example `gpt-6-sol`).\\n- Roles that default to the strongest Claude model (`strongest judgment`): your strongest Codex model (for example `gpt-6-astra`).\\n- Diverse-model panels (`arena`, `architect`, `interrogate`, `how` critics, `reflect`): the adversarial signal comes from model diversity, so use the distinct Codex models available to you. A good default panel on ChatGPT is `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`. If only one model family is reachable, vary reasoning effort and note in the verdict that diversity was reduced.\\n\\n`/setup-pstack` writes the configured model list. On Codex, set it to your Codex model slugs.\\n\\n## Session routing hook\\n\\nThe native pstack plugin bundles the same `SessionStart` routing instruction as the Claude Code plugin, through `session-start.sh` on macOS and Linux and `session-start.ps1` on Windows. Codex runs the hook on startup, resume, clear, and compact after the user trusts the hook through `/hooks`. The hook reads `session hook` from the Codex sheet, at the path in [setup-pstack's runtime table](../../setup-pstack/SKILL.md#other-runtimes); `session hook: off` disables injection.\\n\\nA skills-only installation does not include plugin hooks. Request `poteto-mode` explicitly or add a standing instruction to `AGENTS.md` in that case.\\n\\n## Driver and bundled skills pstack references\\n\\nThe [driver policy](../SKILL.md#non-negotiables) selects the app driver. For skills and drivers named by these workflows, use these Codex equivalents:\\n\\n| Skill or driver named in pstack | On Codex |\\n|---------------------------------|----------|\\n| `run` (drive a CLI/TUI to see a change work) | Run the app yourself via `shell` and observe the real output. |\\n| Project UI driver | Drive the UI with whatever automation you have, or hand the user a concrete manual check. Do not claim done without observing the artifact. |\\n| `plugin-dev:skill-development` (Claude's SKILL.md authoring guidance) | Follow your platform's skill-authoring guidance; the `writing-skills` skill if present. Keep `name` + `description` frontmatter and progressive disclosure. |\\n| `loop` (recurring/self-paced re-invocation, used by `babysit`) | Codex has no `loop` skill. Re-run the step yourself on a cadence, or use a Codex scheduled task if available. |\\n\\n## Per-skill notes\\n\\nAffected skill entry points and the optional Codex slash stubs point here. Most skills need only the tables above. These need one more mapping:\\n\\n| Skill | On Codex |\\n|-------|----------|\\n| `interrogate` | The `subagent_type`/`model`/`readonly` dispatch fields map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models and keep the reviewer panel model-diverse. |\\n| `setup-pstack` | The skill's Other runtimes table names the Codex sheet path and how it loads; the slugs are your Codex models (see Model names above). The role rows are identical. |\\n| `no-comments` | There is no `comment-sicko` subagent type; see Subagent policy above. |\\n| `thermos` | There are no thermo-nuclear subagent types; see Subagent policy above. |\\n| `teach` | Running `how` and `why` in parallel maps to `spawn_agent` fan-out; image generation uses the configured Codex equivalent. |\\n| `create-verification-skill` | The generated skill lands under `.claude/skills/verify/` on Claude Code; write it to Codex's project-skill location instead. The app-driving harness is platform-neutral. |\\n| `maintain-verification-skill` | The parallel per-feature source readers map to `spawn_agent` fan-out; the project-local skill lives under Codex's skills location, not `.claude/skills/`. |\\n| `create-formal-verification-skill` | Write the generated skill to Codex's project-skill location, named for the area it verifies. The verifier and its run command are platform-neutral. |\\n| `maintain-formal-verification-skill` | Project skills, the formal verification skill among them, live under Codex's project-skill location. |\\n| `babysit` | `loop` and `AskUserQuestion` resolve through the tables above. |\\n| `automate-me` | `plugin-dev:skill-development` resolves through the skills table above. |\\n| `architect` | The runner panel goes through the **arena** skill, so its `spawn_agent` fan-out and model substitution apply here too. |\\n| `arena` | The parallel candidates and the cross-judge map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models for the runners and the cross-judge pool (see Model names above). |\\n| `how` | The parallel explorers and the explainer map to `spawn_agent` fan-out; substitute your configured Codex models. |\\n| `reflect` | The three reviewers and the synthesizer map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models. The transcript finder reads Claude Code's layout under `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects/`, so pass the session digest step 1 allows instead. |\\n| `swarm` | Each worker is a `spawn_agent` call on your configured Codex model, and those calls already run concurrently; give each writing worker its own worktree or output directory (see Subagent policy above). |\\n| `why` | The parallel investigators and the synthesizer map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models. List MCP servers from the tools Codex exposes to the session, not from `.mcp.json` or `claude mcp list`. |\\n\\n## Vendored scripts\\n\\n`skills/poteto-mode/scripts/` ships the `watch-pr` PR watcher, the `orch` store CLI, and `worktree-audit.mjs`. The `watch-pr/ship-pr` command owns pending-merge inspection and cancellation; `resume.mjs` owns the shared checkpoint locator described in [Resume storage](resume-storage.md). These scripts use bun and Node.js and run the same on Codex; invoke them through `shell`. They need `bun` and `gh`, or `origin` when the forge is Origin. Run `worktree-audit.mjs` with `node`, as its shebang does. The audit follows each link in a worktree's ancestor directories with `stat` and matches it by the directory it lands on, under bun and node alike. A worktree whose own path bun cannot resolve loses its last chat and lands in `review`. A dangling or looping link is ignored. Any other link that `stat` cannot follow puts every worktree under the link's directory in `review`, with a warning that names the link. `worktree-audit.mjs` scans Codex sessions under `$CODEX_HOME/sessions` and `$CODEX_HOME/archived_sessions` (default `~/.codex`), along with any Claude Code or Pi transcript directory that exists. It imports the transcript walker from `skills/reflect/scripts/find-transcript.mjs`, so keep the `reflect` skill installed beside `poteto-mode`.\\n\\n## Instructions file\\n\\nWhere a pstack skill says \\\"your instructions file\\\", on Codex that is `AGENTS.md` (project root, plus `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` global). On Claude Code it is `CLAUDE.md`.\\n---\\nname: principle-separate-before-serializing-shared-state\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when concurrent actors might write to the same file, branch, key, or state object. Eliminate the sharing first; serialize structurally only when one shared writer is a real invariant.\\\"\\nuser-invocable: false\\n---\\n\\n# Separate Before Serializing Shared State\\n\\nWhen concurrent actors might share mutable state, first ask whether they need the same mutable object. If not, eliminate the sharing. When sharing is real, enforce serialization structurally: lockfiles, sequential phases, exclusive ownership. Instructions and conventions are not concurrency control.\\n\\n**Why:** Concurrent writes to shared state create race conditions that are intermittent, hard to reproduce, and expensive to debug.\\n\\n**Pattern:**\\n1. **Identify shared mutable state** (files both read and write, branches both push to, APIs both define and consume).\\n2. **Default: eliminate the shared write target.** Ask: do these actors need one canonical object, or are they publishing independent facts? Give each actor its own owned file, key, branch, or state directory, and merge only at the read/reporting boundary. Two workers writing their own `lastX` field into one `state.json` is still shared mutation. `indexer-state.json` + `metrics-state.json` is not.\\n3. **Only when one shared write target is a real invariant, serialize access structurally** (lockfiles, sequential phases, single-writer actor, or atomic compare-and-swap). Treat \\\"we need a lock\\\" as a design smell to check, not as the default answer.\\n---\\nname: principle-laziness-protocol\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when refactoring, evaluating diff size, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias toward deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\\\"\\nuser-invocable: false\\n---\\n\\n# Laziness Protocol\\n\\n- **Consolidate decisions.** Do not repeat the same choice in several places. Put it behind one source of truth and pass the result as a simple flag.\\n- **Minimize the diff.** Make the smallest change that solves the problem. Fewer lines beat \\\"elegant\\\" boilerplate.\\n\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792680144.5815861}}
