Multiple LLM reviewers challenge changes from independent angles.\\\"\\n---\\n\\n# Interrogate\\n\\nOn Codex, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOn GitHub Copilot, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/copilot-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nOn Muse, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/muse-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\\n\\nSpawn one reviewer per configured model to adversarially review code changes. The adversarial signal comes from model diversity, not assigned personas. The deliverable is a synthesized verdict. Do NOT auto-apply changes.\\n\\n## Step 1, Scope\\n\\nIdentify what to review from context:\\n\\n- If the user points at specific files or a diff, use that.\\n- If on a feature branch, run `git diff main...HEAD` (or the appropriate base branch) for the full changeset.\\n- If the user's message references recent work, gather the relevant files.\\n\\nPackage the diff or file contents with any surrounding context files the reviewers need to understand the code.\\n\\n## Step 2, Intent\\n\\nBefore spawning reviewers, state the intent in one clear paragraph from the user's message, commit messages, the PR description if one exists, and the code. If you're unsure about the intent, ask the user before proceeding.\\n\\n## Step 3, Spawn Reviewers\\n\\nLaunch all reviewers in a single message with the `Agent` tool, one per entry on the `interrogate reviewers` line in `pstack-models.md`, labeled Reviewer A, B, and so on to match the entry count. If the sheet or that line is missing, use the table defaults.\\n\\n| Subagent | Default model |\\n|----------|---------------|\\n| Reviewer A | `opus` |\\n| Reviewer B | `fable` |\\n| Reviewer C | `sonnet` |\\n| Reviewer D | `haiku` |\\n\\nEach reviewer gets `subagent_type: \\\"general-purpose\\\"`, `readonly: true`, and `model` set to its configured entry, or its table default when there is no configured line. Omit `model` for an `auto` or `inherit-parent` entry, so that reviewer runs on the parent model.\\n\\nIf the `Agent` tool rejects a configured entry, run that reviewer on its family's table default and say so. Families go by model name, such as Opus, Fable, or Sonnet. With no family match, use Reviewer A's default. If it rejects a table default, spawn with the closest equivalent from the valid slugs in its error message (prefer the same family and reasoning tier), and open a separate PR to update the default table. Do not block the review on the slug issue. Never treat an alias entry as a rejected slug or apply either fallback to it.\\n\\nRead `references/reviewer-prompt.md` and fill in the same template for every reviewer with the stated intent, the diff or file contents, the rubric from `references/rubric.md`, and the code-quality lens from `references/code-quality-review.md`. Pick each subagent's reasoning level for the job, per the Subagents section of the **poteto-mode** skill.\\n\\n## Step 4, Synthesize\\n\\nAs results come back, build a unified picture. Parse every reviewer's findings. Merge findings that describe the same issue differently, and note which models raised each one. Findings two or more models raised independently are the highest signal. Read a lone model's finding, but weight it accordingly. Note disagreements. If one model flags something and another explicitly says the opposite, that is context for the verdict.\\n\\n## Step 5, Lead Judgment\\n\\nYou are the lead reviewer, a pragmatic senior engineer, not a neutral aggregator. Read `references/lead-judgment.md` for the full framework. Put every finding in one of four buckets, with the model(s) that raised it and a one-line rationale.\\n\\n- **Act on**. Real issues affecting correctness, security, or maintainability given the actual goals. They would block a real PR.\\n- **Consider**. Legitimate, but you're not sure they outweigh the cost of addressing them now. Worth the user's attention.\\n- **Noted**. Technically valid but not actionable. Context-dependent, premature optimization, or low-impact at this stage.\\n- **Dismissed**. Wrong, nitpicky, or missing context, with a brief reason.\\n\\n## Output Format\\n\\n### Intent\\n> [The stated intent paragraph from Step 2]\\n\\n### Reviewers\\n- Reviewer [label]: [model name], [N findings] (one bullet per reviewer)\\n\\n### Act On\\n[Each: description, which models raised it, why it matters.]\\n\\n### Consider\\n[Each: description, which models raised it, the tradeoff.]\\n\\n### Noted\\n[Brief list.]\\n\\n### Dismissed\\n[Each with a brief rationale.]\\n\\n### Agreement Map\\n[Where models agreed and diverged, and what that pattern tells us.]\\n\\n## Reasoning effort\\n\\nA role value in the override sheet may name a reasoning effort after its model, as in `opus @xhigh`. Levels on Claude Code: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. Which ones apply depends on the model. A value without `@` takes the sheet's `default effort` line, a level or `session`, and `session` when the sheet has no such line. `session` sets no effort, so the dispatch is the usual one. Strip the suffix before reading the model: `inherit-parent` or `auto` still omits `model` at every level, and a model name is passed as `model`. Pass the level as the dispatch's effort field and keep the `subagent_type` you would otherwise use. That is `effort` on the Claude Code `Agent` call and `reasoning_effort` on Codex's `spawn_agent`. On Muse it is `effort` on a Workflow child; a `subagent_spawn` dispatch keeps the session's effort.\\n# Depth consistency design rubric\\n\\nScore0to2 per criterion, choose no design that fails the first two.\\n\\n1. One authoritative solved first-hit point controls depth and water eye attenuation without mixing opaque distance or inventing medium membership.\\n2. Unsovled, air, nonwater and closer opaque/translucent behavior remain explicit and testable; no ghost water or overwritten occluders.\\n3. Same-frame actual depth and post-composite distance/transmission can be observed with exact sampler/state proof and limited diagnostics.\\n4. Small original implementation reuses frozen generators/runtime and keeps all old evidence immutable.\\n5. A fail-first test detects the old depth inconsistency; candidate invariance and negative controls are nonvacuous.\\n6. Root model, shadow/receiver origins, numerical and general-world limits are stated without overclaiming visual or performance completion.\\nimport json\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport unittest\\nimport zipfile\\nimport water_continuous_candidate as candidate\\nimport water_optical_probe as probe\\n\\nROOT=Path('/home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen')\\n\\nclass {"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16412-a15f-7e61-afe9-4276f1d3fd14","call_id":"call_a29be8da09af4ee5a38da8ed64508dab","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.2 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"efa0ce\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000016391,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":2137,\"output\":\"I followed your read-only limits: no subagents, commands or writes. That also means I didn't create a plan file. I read the two design files plus targeted parts of `shaders/composite.fsh`, `shaders/gbuffers_water.fsh` and `shaders/shaders.properties`.\\n\\n## 1. Opaque visibility: pick the unguarded contract\\n\\n**Both straddle cases.** Take an underwater eye, the raster depth `zr`, the solved depth `zo`, and an opaque depth `zp` strictly between them.\\n\\n- **(a) `zr < zp < zo`.** The raster surface is in front of the opaque, but the solved surface is behind it. In the continuous model the opaque point is below the solved interface, so it is underwater and should be seen through water.\\n  - Unguarded: the late test fails, the opaque colour stays, and `depth0 == depth1`. Composite then applies the receiver branch and the eye-to-opaque Beer term. That is consistent with the model.\\n  - Guarded: status 4 draws a raster interface in front of an object the model says is submerged.\\n- **(b) `zo < zp < zr`.** The solved trough is nearer than the opaque. In the model the opaque point is in air above the interface, so water should win.\\n  - Unguarded: the fragment passes, its depth is `zo`, and composite's eye leg ends at the shaded point.\\n  - Guarded: it keeps showing the opaque object, which the model says is hidden.\\n\\n**Verdict.** The guard doesn't repair primary visibility. It keeps the coarse-mesh approximation exactly where the two models disagree. It also adds a new artifact: a contour where status 0 changes to status 4 along every opaque intersection, with optical normals on one side and raster normals on the other.\\n\\nThere is also a structural problem with the guard. It tests `depthtex1`, but the hardware tests the live depth buffer. By the time water draws, that buffer may already hold earlier translucent depths, so the guard isn't even checking the same thing the hardware checks.\\n\\n**Fully blocking ceiling.** This is the vanilla case: water surface at 8/9 of a block with a solid block above, leaving about 0.11 m of headroom. With a larger `heightBound`, the unconstrained wave crosses into the ceiling.\\n- Unguarded shows ceiling patches seen through water. That is what you would see physically in a water–ceiling contact region, where there is no air interface.\\n- The guard hides this by drawing a raster interface, so the pixel claims an air gap the model doesn't have.\\n\\nThe real defect is that the wave isn't contained. That belongs to a later wave-containment or eligibility unit, such as requiring headroom ≥ `heightBound`. It shouldn't be patched per pixel with a visibility guard. Report the straddle counts as a measured symptom of that model domain, not as a pass.\\n\\n**Could anything make the unguarded option unsafe?** Only if, at the water draw, the depth test is off, the depth function isn't LESS or LEQUAL, the depth mask is off, or opaque geometry is missing from the live depth buffer. In every one of those cases, raster visibility was already wrong, and `depthtex1` is a copy of that same buffer, so the guard gives no protection. The one real risk is shader-declared: see below.\\n\\n**Exact pipeline roles:**\\n- **Early fragment tests:** forbid `layout(early_fragment_tests)`. It would test against `gl_FragCoord.z` and drop the write, quietly turning back into raster visibility.\\n- **Conservative depth:** forbid `depth_greater` and `depth_less`, because both directions happen and hi-Z would wrongly cull fragments. Use the default `depth_any`.\\n- **Early-Z cost:** writing depth turns early-Z off for the whole translucent-terrain draw. The existing `discard` at `gbuffers_water.fsh:263` already weakens it.\\n- **Depth writes:** the late test of `gl_FragDepth` against the live buffer chooses the winner. Initialise `gl_FragDepth = gl_FragCoord.z` on every path, including `:261-266`.\\n- **Tie with the opaque:** under LEQUAL, `zo == zp` lets water win while composite's `depth==opaqueDepth` reads it as \\\"no water\\\". Treat exact equality as a representational rejection and count it. This is a tie-break, not a visibility guard.\\n- **Blending:** `blend.gbuffers_water=SRC_ALPHA…` runs only after the depth test passes. A failed fragment changes nothing, including C1 and C3. A passing one blends over whatever opaque colour is already there, which is the correct background in both cases. Data targets (C3, C12–C15) need per-buffer `blend…=off`.\\n- **Primitive coverage:** fragments exist only where the coarse mesh rasterises. `gl_FragDepth` can't create coverage, so silhouettes and crests beyond the mesh edge stay missing. The same-pixel requirement only moves depth along the ray.\\n\\n**Target semantic contract:**\\n> Wherever the mesh covers a pixel, the stored depth is the continuous model's first hit on that pixel ray, and hardware depth ordering decides visibility. Composite's eye leg is reconstructed from that stored depth.\\n\\nThis matches the parent's preference. Proof must come from native straddle tests in both directions, which must fail on hit1, plus actual evidence; see the open gates.\\n\\n## 2. Reflected-source `incidentDepth`: defer it\\n\\n`gbuffers_water.fsh:442` (and `:408`) use `absolutePosition.y` as the height of the Sun's entry interface. The Sun actually enters the surface somewhere above the receiver, at a different xz. So neither the raster Y nor the optical Y at this pixel is correct. Moving it would swap one wrong local height for another, change the output, and muddy what the depth unit can be credited with.\\n\\n**Smallest coherent unit:**\\n- primary `opticalPoint`, which is already in hit1;\\n- `gl_FragDepth`, which composite's eye Beer term picks up through `depth0` with no code change;\\n- the `straightPath`/`initialPath` origin;\\n- the `shadowVisibility` point;\\n- reflection and refraction origins, which already moved in hit1.\\n\\nKeep raster `footprint`, derivatives, eligibility, `skyAccess`/`lightmap`, and the trace `surfaceY` arguments, which don't apply underwater. Label `:442`/`:408` honestly as \\\"source-radiance interface height = raster plane (approximation, R2 scope)\\\".\\n\\n## 3. Observation schema: matrix readback isn't needed\\n\\nDistance is the only quantity composite takes from depth underwater. Two baseline-plus-candidate loops close the chain using the actual uniforms in each program:\\n\\n1. **Baseline (raster depth):** C2.x = |C1.xyz| for all water pixels. This proves composite's actual inverse projection matches the hardware raster transform, and that the eye is at the relative-world origin. A bobbing or translation offset would show up as a systematic, falsifiable mismatch.\\n2. **Candidate:** `depth0` = C3.w, within 0 for D32F or 1 quantum for D24, using the format actually read. This shows the written depth is the computed one. Then C2.x = |C3.xyz| shows that depth reconstructs the point's distance, and C2.yzw = `waterTransmission(C2.x)`.\\n\\nDirection is only covered by the shader's own same-pixel check, so it rests on the native fixture. That is a small, honestly labelled gap, not a meaningful one.\\n\\n**Straddle detection needs no extra channel:**\\n- (a) baseline C3.w > `depth1` at pixels where the raster is in front;\\n- (b) pixels where the candidate has C3 coverage and the baseline doesn't.\\n\\nThis requires a known per-frame clear sentinel on C3.\\n\\n**Schema:**\\n- **C1:** raster point, unchanged.\\n- **C3.xyz:** `opticalPoint`.\\n- **C3.w:** projected depth, −1 if ineligible, −2 if solved but rejected (projection or tie).\\n- **C2:** composite's `(distanceToEye, transmission)`.\\n- **Also capture:** raw `depth0`/`depth1` (strict depth-format sampler), all from the same FINAL.\\n- **Byte-identical checks against baseline:** `depth1`, `depth0` outside status 0, and C1.\\n\\n## Open gates (none passed by this unit)\\n- Actual hardware depth state during the water draw (DEPTH_TEST, FUNC, WRITEMASK). Prove it with a read-only `glGet`, or leave it open.\\n- Actual straddles in both directions, and the ceiling case. Report `null` if no Lab geometry falls in the band.\\n- Wave containment or headroom, i.e. contact physics.\\n- Later translucents, particles, hand and clouds interacting with the new depth.\\n- In-game evidence for the shadow-point move; for now it is checked only by source diff and native tests.\\n- The `:442`/`:408` source-radiance surface height.\\n- Coverage beyond the coarse mesh.\\n- Early-Z cost.\\n- Beyond this frozen setup (fixed phase, zero ripples, deep water, limited footprint): shallow, shore, cave and unknown worlds; R2; physical, visual and release acceptance.\\n\\nNothing here claims that all curl or visibility errors are solved.\\n\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792680304.9911215}}

{"type":"custom_tool_call","id":"ctc_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada21adc72487d09e2bf2c1da4d921f","status":"completed","call_id":"call_86659663b5f247939e9700bfd2fa3dac","name":"exec","input":"text(await tools.apply_patch(\"*** Begin Patch\\n*** Add File: /home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen/reports/2026-10-22-water-hit-depth/ARCHITECTURE.md\\n+# First-hit depth consistency contract\\n+\\n+The accepted primary water hit is one record containing point, view point, projected window depth and acceptance. Eligibility and derivatives remain raster-owned. Accepted hits write their projected depth; hardware late depth ordering selects visibility. Composite owns one eye attenuation operation reconstructed from the surviving depth. No opaque-depth guard preserves the old raster winner.\\n+\\n+## Synthesis\\n+\\n+The independent Claude and Codex designs both prefer hardware depth over a new transport-only MRT. Parent scores against `.audit/depth-arena-rubric-2026-10-22.md` are Codex (2,2,2,2,2,1) and original Claude (1,1,2,2,2,2). The original Claude opaque guard fails the mandatory visibility criterion. Actual Claude crossjudge withdrew that guard and agrees with the Codex visibility contract. Its evidence is `claude-crossjudge/RESULT.md`.\\n+\\n+Graft Claude's baseline-plus-candidate observation chain and source-radiance scope limit. Do not move reflected-source incidentDepth in this unit. That existing raster-plane solar-entry approximation is R2 work, not repaired physical transport. Do not change wave evaluation, IOR, Fresnel, exposure, raster eligibility, trace surface-Y approximations or composite lighting at the eye.\\n+\\n+## Caller and data shape\\n+\\n+```glsl\\n+struct WaterPrimaryHit {\\n+    bool accepted;\\n+    vec3 point;\\n+    vec3 viewPoint;\\n+    float windowDepth;\\n+};\\n+WaterPrimaryHit waterPrimaryHit(vec3 rasterPoint, vec3 solvedPoint, int solveStatus) {\\n+    // not implemented in this sketch\\n+}\\n+```\\n+\\n+At main entry initialize gl_FragDepth to gl_FragCoord.z on every path. A validated solved point overrides it. Require finite point/clip values, positive clip W, projected XY within 0.01 pixel of gl_FragCoord, and strictly in-range window depth computed using gl_DepthRange. Invalid projection retains raster point/shading/depth. No early_fragment_tests or conservative depth declaration. Exact opaque ties/quantization require observation and remain an explicit representational gate, not a raster-preserving occlusion guard.\\n+\\n+Update initial straightPath from opticalViewPos and shadowVisibility from opticalPoint for accepted hits. Preserve all raster derivatives. Existing optical trace origins stay at the hit. Composite C0 formula is unchanged, but its input depth is corrected. No new producer or simulation writes.\\n+\\n+## Isolated artifacts\\n+\\n+The writer owns only new generator/helper/tests in `/tmp/aqualumen-depth-writer.lIYL1g`. Parent integrates after review and owns native tests, actual GPU/Minecraft lifecycle, qualification and restoration. Original shader sources, hit1 archives, observers and Evidence stay immutable. New outputs use exclusive creation and record every input/generated source hash.\\n+\\n+Generate hit1d baseline and depth1 candidate from pinned hit1 control/paired archives. Baseline shading/depth remains hit1. Both paired packs add passive observation. C1 keeps raster point/mean. C3 becomes opticalPoint.xyz plus projected accepted depth, -1 ineligible, -2 projection rejected. C2 is post-composite actual distanceToEye/transmission RGB, with finite zero transmission outside the underwater branch. C12 and C13-C15 retain meanings. New observer schema/version explicitly replaces geometry/status meanings.\\n+\\n+Keep depth0/1 active only in FINAL preview branches, with actual capture preview zero. Read single-component float depth with strict allowed depth formats, dimensions/base level and comparison mode; never weaken the RGBA32F color boundary. Preserve GL read state and verify all sampler bindings before/after FINAL. Exact source/program/pack/raw hashes bind analysis to captures.\\n+\\n+## Verification\\n+\\n+Native eight-MRT fixture attaches real D32F depth. Unobstructed hit1 must fail optical point/depth consistency, candidate must pass. Both opaque straddle directions must be exercised and corrected, plus a fully blocking ceiling. Render actual generated composite for eye distance and one Beer term. Native passivity compares each instrumented copy with its own control, not candidate with original C0. Native compile is not Iris attachment proof.\\n+\\n+Actual same-FINAL near/deep captures add previously absent depth data. Baseline must match composite distance to raster and fail agreement with optical point. Candidate must match emitted projected depth to raw depth0, optical point distance to composite distance and transmission to Beer. Compare C1/depth1 byte-identically where scene stability supports it. Use format- and distance-conditioned numerical tolerances, not an unexplained global relaxation.\\n+\\n+Actual draw-state/opaque straddles, coverage outside raster mesh, headroom/contact containment, later translucents/hand, shadow radiance attribution, R2 source height, dynamic ripples, general worlds, visual acceptance and performance remain separately open unless observed. Test-only success is not production completion. Restore Lab 0.6/physics 0.2 and prove configs/mods/ordinary save unchanged at closure.\\n*** End Patch\"));\ntext(await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy /home/kusutohu/.codex/plugins/cache/pstack-claude/pstack/0.9.101/skills/show-me-your-work/scripts/log.sh .audit/depth-consistency-2026-10-22.tsv design 'Selected continuous primary hardware depth with late visibility' 'Claude crossjudge rejected its original raster-preserving opaque guard; both directions need the solved surface' reports/2026-10-22-water-hit-depth/ARCHITECTURE.md 'contract frozen; implementation next'\",\"max_output_tokens\":1000}));\n","internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792680365.7816663}}

saved water-interface vectors against Snell/Fresnel and fixed image ROIs.\\\"\\\"\\\"\\nimport argparse\\nfrom array import array\\nimport hashlib\\nimport json\\nimport math\\nfrom pathlib import Path\\nimport sys\\n\\nROIS = {\\n    'near-tongue': (1350, 120, 1510, 300),\\n    'top-rim': (40, 0, 400, 180),\\n    'interior': (700, 350, 900, 550),\\n    'exterior': (1500, 20, 1600, 120),\\n    'deep-top-loop': (1380, 0, 1440, 60),\\n    'deep-right-loop': (1380, 600, 1460, 700),\\n    'deep-left-loop': (150, 600, 320, 800),\\n}\\n\\n\\ndef require(ok, message):\\n    if not ok:\\n        raise ValueError(message)\\n\\n\\ndef snell(normal, view, ratio=1.333):\\n    ci = max(0.0, min(1.0, sum(n*v for n, v in zip(normal, view))))\\n    k = 1.0 - ratio*ratio*(1.0-ci*ci)\\n    if k <= 0:\\n        return k, 1.0, (0.0, 0.0, 0.0)\\n    ct = math.sqrt(k)\\n    rs = (ratio*ci-ct)/(ratio*ci+ct)\\n    rp = (ci-ratio*ct)/(ci+ratio*ct)\\n    direction = tuple(-ratio*v + (ratio*ci-ct)*n for n, v in zip(normal, view))\\n    return k, 0.5*(rs*rs+rp*rp), direction\\n\\n\\ndef load(directory):\\n    m = json.loads((directory / 'manifest.json').read_text())\\n    require(m['available'] and m['beforeReadStateRestored'] and m['afterReadStateRestored']\\n            and m['quadInvocationReturnedObserved'] and m['previewUniform'] == 0, 'Pairing unavailable')\\n    w, h = m['width'], m['height']\\n    data = {}\\n    for key in ('classification', 'sourceC0', *('optics-colortex'+str(i) for i in (1,2,3,13,14,15))):\\n        entry = m['files'][key]\\n        require(Path(entry['name']).name == entry['name'], 'Unsafe raw path')\\n        path = directory / entry['name']\\n        raw = path.read_bytes()\\n        require(len(raw) == w*h*16 and len(raw) == entry['bytes']\\n                and hashlib.sha256(raw).hexdigest() == entry['sha256'], 'Raw identity/extent mismatch')\\n        values = array('f')\\n        values.frombytes(raw)\\n        if sys.byteorder != 'little':\\n            values.byteswap()\\n        data[key] = values\\n    return m, data\\n\\n\\ndef analyze(directory):\\n    m, data = load(directory)\\n    w, h = m['width'], m['height']\\n    c, n, v, t = (data[k] for k in ('classification', 'optics-colortex13', 'optics-colortex14', 'optics-colortex15'))\\n    geometry, before, position, color = (data[k] for k in ('optics-colortex2', 'optics-colortex3', 'optics-colortex1', 'sourceC0'))\\n    result = dict(scene=m['scene'], frame=m['frame'], manifestSHA256=hashlib.sha256((directory/'manifest.json').read_bytes()).hexdigest(),\\n                  maxDiscriminantError=0.0, maxDirectionErrorAwayFromCritical=0.0,\\n                  maxFresnelErrorAwayFromCritical=0.0, maxNormalBlendError=0.0,\\n                  transmitting=0, downwardTransmitting=0, screenReceiverDownward=0, criticalUncertain=0)\\n    for at in range(0, w*h*4, 4):\\n        if c[at+3] != 1:\\n            continue\\n        require(all(math.isfinite(a[at+j]) for a in (c,n,v,t,geometry,before,position,color) for j in range(4)), 'Nonfinite input')\\n        normal, view = tuple(n[at:at+3]), tuple(v[at:at+3])\\n        require(abs(sum(x*x for x in normal)-1) < 2e-5 and abs(sum(x*x for x in view)-1) < 2e-5, 'Nonunit normal/view')\\n        k, f, ray = snell(normal, view)\\n        result['maxDiscriminantError'] = max(result['maxDiscriminantError'], abs(k-n[at+3]))\\n        if abs(k) <= 1e-5:\\n            result['criticalUncertain'] += 1\\n        else:\\n            result['maxFresnelErrorAwayFromCritical'] = max(result['maxFresnelErrorAwayFromCritical'], abs(f-c[at+1]))\\n            result['maxDirectionErrorAwayFromCritical'] = max(result['maxDirectionErrorAwayFromCritical'], max(abs(ray[j]-t[at+j]) for j in range(3)))\\n        flags = geometry[at+3]\\n        require(flags == int(flags) and 0 <= flags <= 3, 'Invalid interface flags')\\n        blend = before[at+3]\\n        require(0 <= blend <= 1, 'Invalid grazing blend')\\n        if int(flags) & 1:\\n            mixed = tuple(geometry[at+j]*(1-blend)+before[at+j]*blend for j in range(3))\\n            length = math.sqrt(sum(x*x for x in mixed))\\n            require(length > 0, 'Degenerate blend')\\n            result['maxNormalBlendError'] = max(result['maxNormalBlendError'], max(abs(mixed[j]/length-normal[j]) for j in range(3)))\\n        if c[at+1] < .9999:\\n            result['transmitting'] += 1\\n            if t[at+1] < 0:\\n                result['downwardTransmitting'] += 1\\n                result['screenReceiverDownward'] += int(t[at+3] == 1)\\n    require(result['maxDiscriminantError'] < 1e-5, 'Snell discriminant disagreement')\\n    require(result['maxFresnelErrorAwayFromCritical'] < .005, 'Fresnel disagreement away from critical band')\\n    require(result['maxDirectionErrorAwayFromCritical'] < .005, 'Refraction disagreement away from critical band')\\n    require(result['maxNormalBlendError'] < 2e-5, 'Actual normal differs from recorded grazing blend')\\n    regions = {}\\n    for name, box in ROIS.items():\\n        counts = dict(water=0, transmitting=0, downward=0, tir=0, grazingChanged=0, screenReceiver=0)\\n        for display_y in range(max(0, box[1]), min(h, box[3])):\\n            for x in range(max(0, box[0]), min(w, box[2])):\\n                at = ((h-1-display_y)*w+x)*4\\n                if c[at+3] != 1:\\n                    continue\\n                counts['water'] += 1\\n                counts['grazingChanged'] += int(before[at+3] < 1)\\n                if c[at+1] < .9999:\\n                    counts['transmitting'] += 1\\n                    counts['downward'] += int(t[at+1] < 0)\\n                    counts['screenReceiver'] += int(t[at+3] == 1)\\n                else:\\n                    counts['tir'] += 1\\n        counts['downwardFractionOfTransmitting'] = counts['downward']/max(1,counts['transmitting'])\\n        regions[name] = dict(displayBox=list(box), counts=counts)\\n    result['regions'] = regions\\n    result['sample1430_200'] = {}\\n    if w > 1430 and h > 200:\\n        at = ((h-1-200)*w+1430)*4\\n        result['sample1430_200'] = {key:list(values[at:at+4]) for key,values in data.items()}\\n    result['interpretationLimits'] = ['ROIs were declared before numeric inspection; boxes are not annotated fold-pixel masks.',\\n        'Direction agreement and a downward ray do not establish physical secondary water-surface visibility.',\\n        'No sufficient-cause decision from ROI percentage alone; compare images and radiometric source before a fix.',\\n        'C0 may include later translucent draws while optical records describe surviving water draws.']\\n    return result\\n\\n\\ndef main():\\n    parser = argparse.ArgumentParser(description=__doc__)\\n    parser.add_argument('capture', type=Path)\\n    parser.add_argument('output', type=Path)\\n    args = parser.parse_args()\\n    result = Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\\\"\\nuser-invocable: false\\n---\\n\\n# Explain the Number\\n\\n- **Ask \\\"why not double?\\\"** Name the resource or code path that bounds the result, such as a core, a lock, the disk, the network, or the load generator itself. Get it from a profile or from system counters taken during a run, then map it to source. A guess from reading the code is not a limiter. If you cannot say why the number is not twice as good, you do not know what you measured.\\n- **List what else the number could be measuring, and rule out each one with evidence.** The usual suspects are errors, skipped or cached work, code that never ran, an untuned side (one left on default settings), noise, and a piece too small to matter end to end.\\n- **Keep the evidence with the number.** Put the run count, the spread, and the limiter in the notes or a linked artifact.\\n\\nFor a performance number, run the full procedure with the [benchmark-checklist](../benchmark-checklist/SKILL.md) skill. For an eval result, ask the same of the trials: did every run do the task, does the gap hold across trials and models, and does the scenario matter.\\n\\nYou skipped this when the evidence behind a number has no run count, no spread, or no named limiter, or when the time saved is larger than the time the changed piece took.\\n\\nDistinct from [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md), which checks that an output is real.\\n\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792680823.9505575}}

{"type":"agent_message","id":"amsg_01a1641e-062b-76b0-9dbe-946e1afa54e4","author":"/root/depth_fixture_diagnosis","recipient":"/root","content":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER\nTask name: /root\nSender: /root/depth_fixture_diagnosis\nPayload:\n原因は深度テクスチャではなく、`water_paired_native.cpp:78` の逆射影行列です。初期値が15要素しかなく、1個の `0` が欠けています。\n\n現在：\n\n```cpp\n{1/f,0,0,0,0,1/f,0,0,0,0,1/b,0,0,-1,a/b}\n```\n\n正しくは：\n\n```cpp\n{1/f,0,0,0,0,1/f,0,0,0,0,0,1/b,0,0,-1,a/b}\n```\n\n末尾の暗黙ゼロにより `viewPosition()` の除数 `p.w` が常に0になり、距離が無限大 → `min(length(view), far)` で128に張り付きます。透過率が128と整合する現象も説明できます。\n\n既存Evidenceを保持するなら、新しい `DepthFixture` 内で正しい逆行列を設定し、GPU実行前に `projection × inverseProjection ≈ identity` を検査してください。読み取りのみで、編集・GPU実行はしていません。"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792681051.6917439}}

{"type":"custom_tool_call","id":"ctc_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada24a728c887d091db9d6faa8d77a7","status":"completed","call_id":"call_8f71da61675c401fbb54dca66c51837a","name":"exec","input":"text(await tools.apply_patch(\"*** Begin Patch\\n*** Add File: /home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen/tools/water_hit_depth_audit.py\\n+import argparse\\n+import hashlib\\n+import json\\n+from pathlib import Path\\n+\\n+import numpy as np\\n+\\n+WORLD='b22dbccb-2970-41e6-847f-f183b1ec923d'\\n+DISTANCE_BUDGET=0.001\\n+BEER_BUDGET=1e-6\\n+\\n+\\n+def sha(data):\\n+    return hashlib.sha256(data).hexdigest()\\n+\\n+\\n+def load(directory):\\n+    manifest_path=directory/'manifest.json'\\n+    manifest=json.loads(manifest_path.read_text())\\n+    assert manifest['worldId']==WORLD and 'NVIDIA' in manifest['glRenderer']\\n+    assert manifest['opticalSchema']=='aqualumen-water-hit-depth-v1'\\n+    assert all(manifest[key] is True for key in ('available','beforeReadStateRestored','afterReadStateRestored',\\n+        'quadInvocationReturnedObserved','opticalSamplerMapsMatchedAfterQuad','depthSamplerMapsMatchedAfterQuad'))\\n+    assert manifest['previewUniform']==0\\n+    assert manifest['opticalChannels']['colortex2']==['distanceToEye','transmission.r','transmission.g','transmission.b']\\n+    assert manifest['opticalChannels']['colortex3']==['opticalPoint.x','opticalPoint.y','opticalPoint.z','projectedDepthOrNegativeStatus']\\n+    w,h=manifest['width'],manifest['height'];data={}\\n+    for key,entry in manifest['files'].items():\\n+        assert Path(entry['name']).name==entry['name']\\n+        raw=(directory/entry['name']).read_bytes()\\n+        assert len(raw)==entry['bytes'] and sha(raw)==entry['sha256']\\n+        if key.startswith('depth-') or key.startswith('optics-') or key=='classification':\\n+            components=1 if key.startswith('depth-') else 4\\n+            assert len(raw)==w*h*4*components\\n+            data[key]=np.frombuffer(raw,dtype='<f4').reshape(h,w,components).astype(np.float64)\\n+            assert np.isfinite(data[key]).all()\\n+    colors=[manifest[key] for key in ('c0Sampler','c12Sampler')]+list(manifest['opticalSamplers'].values())\\n+    depths=list(manifest['depthSamplers'].values())\\n+    assert all(s['format']==34836 and s['baseLevel']==0 and s['width']==w and s['height']==h for s in colors)\\n+    assert len({s['texture'] for s in colors+depths})==len(colors+depths)\\n+    assert all(s['format'] in (33190,36012,35056,36013) and s['baseLevel']==0 and s['width']==w and s['height']==h\\n+        and s['compareMode']==0 and s['effectiveCompareMode']==0 for s in depths)\\n+    assert all(((data['depth-'+name]>=0)&(data['depth-'+name]<=1)).all() for name in ('depthtex0','depthtex1'))\\n+    return manifest,data,sha(manifest_path.read_bytes())\\n+\\n+\\n+def distribution(values,mask):\\n+    selected=np.asarray(values)[mask]\\n+    return dict(count=int(selected.size),p50=float(np.quantile(selected,.5)),p95=float(np.quantile(selected,.95)),\\n+        p99=float(np.quantile(selected,.99)),maximum=float(np.max(selected))) if selected.size else dict(count=0)\\n+\\n+\\n+def analyze(directory,mode,baseline=None):\\n+    m,d,manifest_hash=load(directory)\\n+    assert m['observerVersion']==('0.1.0-hit1d' if mode=='baseline' else '0.1.0-depth1')\\n+    assert m['depthCandidateWritesAcceptedHit']==(mode=='candidate')\\n+    water=d['classification'][...,3]==1\\n+    raster=d['optics-colortex1'][...,:3];point=d['optics-colortex3'][...,:3]\\n+    expected_depth=d['optics-colortex3'][...,3]\\n+    accepted=water&(expected_depth>=0)\\n+    assert accepted.sum()>1_000_000\\n+    distance=d['optics-colortex2'][...,0]\\n+    raster_distance=np.linalg.norm(raster,axis=-1);point_distance=np.linalg.norm(point,axis=-1)\\n+    distance_error=np.abs(distance-(raster_distance if mode=='baseline' else np.where(accepted,point_distance,raster_distance)))\\n+    point_error=np.abs(distance-point_distance)\\n+    depth=d['depth-depthtex0'][...,0];opaque=d['depth-depthtex1'][...,0]\\n+    storage=m['depthSamplers']['depthtex0']['format']\\n+    depth_tolerance=1.05/(2**24-1) if storage in (33190,35056) else 0.0\\n+    depth_error=np.abs(depth-expected_depth)\\n+    transmission=d['optics-colortex2'][...,1:]\\n+    beer=np.exp(-distance[...,None]*np.array([.32,.085,.035]))\\n+    beer_error=np.max(np.abs(transmission-beer),axis=-1)\\n+    result=dict(schema='aqualumen-bounded-water-hit-depth-audit-v1',mode=mode,scene=m['scene'],\\n+        auditScriptSHA256=sha(Path(__file__).read_bytes()),numpyVersion=np.__version__,manifestSHA256=manifest_hash,\\n+        capture=str(directory),packSHA256=m['packSha256'],renderer=m['glRenderer'],pixels=int(water.size),\\n+        waterPixels=int(water.sum()),acceptedPixels=int(accepted.sum()),ineligibleOrUnsolved=int((water&(expected_depth==-1)).sum()),\\n+        projectionRejected=int((water&(expected_depth==-2)).sum()),depthFormat=storage,\\n+        distanceBudgetMeters=DISTANCE_BUDGET,beerAbsoluteBudget=BEER_BUDGET,storedDepthTolerance=depth_tolerance,\\n+        distanceErrorMeters=distribution(distance_error,water),opticalPointDistanceErrorMeters=distribution(point_error,accepted),\\n+        storedVsEmittedDepthError=distribution(depth_error,accepted),beerAbsoluteError=distribution(beer_error,water),\\n+        distanceViolations=int((water&(distance_error>DISTANCE_BUDGET)).sum()),\\n+        opticalPointDistanceViolations=int((accepted&(point_error>DISTANCE_BUDGET)).sum()),\\n+        storedDepthViolations=int((accepted&(depth_error>depth_tolerance)).sum()),\\n+        beerViolations=int((water&(beer_error>BEER_BUDGET)).sum()),\\n+        emittedOpaqueExactTies=int((accepted&(expected_depth==opaque)).sum()),\\n+        emittedOpaqueWithinOneQuantum=int((accepted&(np.abs(expected_depth-opaque)<=max(depth_tolerance,2**-24))).sum()),\\n+        opticalMinusRasterDistanceMeters=distribution(point_distance-raster_distance,accepted),\\n+        absoluteOpticalMinusRasterDistanceMeters=distribution(np.abs(point_distance-raster_distance),accepted),\\n+        actualDrawDepthStateObserved=False,actualOpaqueStraddleExercised=None)\\n+    result['boundedDepthEyeConsistencyPassed']=bool(result['distanceViolations']==0 and result['beerViolations']==0\\n+        and (result['storedDepthViolations']==0 if mode=='candidate' else result['storedDepthViolations']>1000 and result['opticalPointDistanceViolations']>1000))\\n+    if baseline is not None:\\n+        bm,bd,bhash=load(baseline)\\n+        assert bm['scene']==m['scene'] and bm['width']==m['width'] and bm['height']==m['height']\\n+        mutual=accepted&(bd['classification'][...,3]==1)&(bd['optics-colortex3'][...,3]>=0)\\n+        result['baselineManifestSHA256']=bhash\\n+        result['mutuallyVisibleAcceptedPixels']=int(mutual.sum())\\n+        result['rasterBytesExact']=np.array_equal(d['optics-colortex1'],bd['optics-colortex1'])\\n+        result['opaqueDepthBytesExact']=np.array_equal(d['depth-depthtex1'],bd['depth-depthtex1'])\\n+        result['viewBytesExact']=np.array_equal(d['optics-colortex14'],bd['optics-colortex14'])\\n+        result['opticalPointDifferenceMeters']=distribution(np.linalg.norm(point-bd['optics-colortex3'][...,:3],axis=-1),mutual)\\n+        result['normalDifference']=distribution(np.linalg.norm(d['optics-colortex13'][...,:3]-bd['optics-colortex13'][...,:3],axis=-1),mutual)\\n+        result['fresnelDifference']=distribution(np.abs(d['classification'][...,1]-bd['classification'][...,1]),mutual)\\n+        result['depthChangedPixels']=int((depth!=bd['depth-depthtex0'][...,0]).sum())\\n+        result['visibleWaterChangedPixels']=int(((d['classification'][...,3]==1)!=(bd['classification'][...,3]==1)).sum())\\n+    result['openGates']=[\\n+        'Millimeter-scale point distance, not two-ULP root/projection acceptance. Eye-relative origin is checked by the baseline raster chain, not a captured matrix.',\\n+        'Native opaque straddles do not establish actual Iris water draw state or actual straddle/ceiling behavior.',\\n+        'Later translucency/hand ownership, raster coverage, headroom containment, dynamic ripples and general worlds remain open.',\\n+        'Source incident height, shadow radiance attribution, R2, complete physical/visual/performance and release remain open.',\\n+        'Live clouds and non-wave time prevent cross-run beauty/C0 equality; no radiance improvement inferred.']\\n+    return result\\n+\\n+\\n+if __name__=='__main__':\\n+    parser=argparse.ArgumentParser()\\n+    parser.add_argument('capture',type=Path)\\n+    parser.add_argument('--mode',choices=('baseline','candidate'),required=True)\\n+    parser.add_argument('--baseline',type=Path)\\n+    parser.add_argument('--out',type=Path,required=True)\\n+    args=parser.parse_args()\\n+    result=analyze(args.capture,args.mode,args.baseline)\\n+    with args.out.open('x') as stream:\\n+        json.dump(result,stream,indent=2)\\n+    print(json.dumps({key:result[key] for key in ('mode','scene','waterPixels','acceptedPixels','distanceViolations',\\n+        'storedDepthViolations','beerViolations','boundedDepthEyeConsistencyPassed')}))\\n+    raise SystemExit(0 if result['boundedDepthEyeConsistencyPassed'] else 1)\\n*** End Patch\"));\ntext(await tools.write_stdin({session_id:29152,chars:\"\",max_output_tokens:1500,yield_time_ms:1000}));\n","internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792681127.171092}}

## 2026-10-22T15:08:08.255Z source line 59076

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16427-bcbf-7570-8f3f-018dcdd8e0b3","call_id":"call_bfbd11523e0146b7af41373ef2ce4fc0","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.7 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"5083a8\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000013355,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1983,\"output\":\"# Correctness review: water hit-depth TEST unit\\n\\nI wrote none of these files. I ran nothing: no commands, GPU, game or network. All native numbers below are as you reported them. I did 7 file reads plus targeted greps. I verified nothing at runtime.\\n\\n## Confirmed correct (no issue)\\n\\n- **Window depth formula** (`water_hit_depth_test.glsl:16`): matches the GL viewport transform. Fallback depth is `gl_FragCoord.z`, set first thing in `main` (`candidate-passivity/paired/shaders/gbuffers_water.fsh:323`), before the early `return` at :337. So every path writes depth.\\n- **Fallback view point**: when the hit is not accepted, `opticalViewPos` is `gbufferModelView*worldPosition`, the same expression hit1 used when `opticalPoint==worldPosition` (:367–378, :410). Initial `straightPath` and `shadowVisibility(opticalPoint,n)` change only for accepted hits (:411–413, :435).\\n- **`DepthFixture` inverse** (`water_hit_depth_native.cpp:16–19`): correct for a column-major perspective matrix, and the product check really computes P·P⁻¹.\\n- **Straddle logic** (`native.cpp:141–162`): the opaque depth is placed at the midpoint, so hit1 must be wrong in both directions and the candidate must store the expected depth. The ceiling test (:163–164) is sound.\\n- **Blending**: off for every paired MRT (`shaders.properties:18,33–38,40`). No control pass mentions `colortex2`, so reusing C2 in composite has no consumer that would be disturbed.\\n- **Depth reader**: the GL enum constants check out. It checks format, base level and comparison mode, and restores active unit, binding and pack state via `preserving`. Aliasing is checked against color samplers and the output attachment.\\n\\n## Issues, ordered by severity\\n\\n### 1. High (can decide the verdict): the fixed 1 mm budget is below the depth-storage error floor at moderate distance\\nComposite rebuilds distance from a single depth value (`composite.fsh:24–27`). The distance error from one depth step is about δd ≈ d²/n · ulp(w).\\n\\n- With Minecraft's n = 0.05 and ulp ≈ 2⁻²⁴ near w = 1, one depth step equals **1 mm at d ≈ 29 m**. Half a step reaches 1 mm at about 41 m. D24 and D32F behave alike here.\\n- Your native maximum of 2.13e-5 m at a fixture distance of a few metres fits this d² scaling.\\n- In an underwater pose, the surface raster can reach far toward the horizon or render distance. If so, `distanceViolations` will be non-zero in both modes from storage precision alone (`water_hit_depth_audit.py:64,81,90–91`).\\n- The baseline pass condition requires `distanceViolations==0`. Your \\\"matrix readback omitted\\\" caveat depends on the baseline chain passing, so this condition can fail for reasons unrelated to the eye-origin question.\\n- `ARCHITECTURE.md:41` promises \\\"format- and distance-conditioned numerical tolerances\\\", but :43 and the audit use one global 1 mm. That contradiction is a documentation overclaim.\\n\\n**To reproduce:** for violating pixels, plot `distanceErrorMeters` against `point_distance`. They should follow d²/n·2⁻²⁴.\\n\\n**Action:** do not relax the pre-declared budget after the fact. Report the maximum accepted distance and a per-pixel floor alongside the violations. If violations sit beyond about 29 m and match the floor, record the result as a failure of the declared budget, not a contract bug and not a pass. Fix the wording at :41.\\n\\n### 2. Medium (TEST safety): `stopped()` now fails open\\n- **Before:** `display_water_runtime.py:44–46` refused if *any* `java` process was running.\\n- **Now:** `water_hit_depth_runtime.py:9–24,35–36` only blocks a Java process whose `readlink(cwd)` equals `GAME` exactly. It compares unresolved paths (`:18`).\\n\\nIf the live Lab JVM's cwd differs textually, the check reports \\\"stopped\\\" while Lab is running. Causes include a symlinked or bind-mounted home under Flatpak, a launcher working-directory override, or a `\\\" (deleted)\\\"` suffix. `water_paired_runtime.py:120–150` would then write into `mods/` and `shaderpacks/` of a live client.\\n\\n**To reproduce:** start a same-user Java process with cwd at a symlink alias of `GAME`, then call `stopped()`. It passes.\\n\\n**Action:** fail closed. Resolve both sides, and refuse if any same-user `java` has `GAME` in `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` or an open fd under `GAME`.\\n\\n### 3. Medium: exact ties with opaque depth send visible water through the opaque caustic branch, and the verdict ignores them\\n- Iris draws water with LEQUAL, so a tie lets the water fragment win.\\n- `composite.fsh:37` then sees `depth==opaqueDepth` and adds opaque-receiver Sun light (`colortex8`) onto a water pixel.\\n- Ties become more likely once projected depth is written near contacts.\\n- The audit counts `emittedOpaqueExactTies` (`audit.py:85`) but leaves it out of `boundedDepthEyeConsistencyPassed` (:90–91) and out of `openGates` (:106–111).\\n\\n**Action:** either fail when ties > 0 or list ties in `openGates`. Do not report a pass as tie-safe.\\n\\n### 4. Low: the \\\"effective\\\" comparison-mode check may be vacuous\\n`DEPTH_READER` (`water_depth_candidate.py:100`) reads `GL_SAMPLER_BINDING` through `glGetIntegeri`. As far as I know, core GL treats `SAMPLER_BINDING` as per-active-unit `glGetIntegerv` state, not an indexed target. If so, the call may raise `INVALID_ENUM` and return 0. That would make `effectiveCompareMode` silently equal the texture's own mode.\\n\\nThe same pattern was inherited at `GlReadback.java:51,68`. Please confirm on NVIDIA: after `active(unit)`, call `glGetIntegerv(35097)` and then check `glGetError`. The claim at `ARCHITECTURE.md:35` depends on this.\\n\\n### 5. Low: depth-stencil formats are accepted without recording the stencil sampling mode\\nFormats 35056 and 36013 are allowed (`:98`), but `GL_DEPTH_STENCIL_TEXTURE_MODE` (37098) is not recorded. Raw `glGetTexImage(DEPTH_COMPONENT)` is unaffected. Composite's `texelFetch` would return the wrong data if that mode were STENCIL. Record it.\\n\\n### 6. Low: the `accepted` mask includes 0.0\\n`accepted=water&(expected_depth>=0)` (`audit.py:60`) counts the `vec4(0.0)` written on non-underwater paths. The helper only produces values strictly inside (0,1). This fails safe today, since the Beer and stored-depth checks catch it, but `>0` matches the contract.\\n\\n### 7. Low: the capture is not checked against the generated pack hash\\nThe audit records `packSha256` (:75) but never compares it with `archives.paired` in `<mode>/manifest.json`. `configure()` sets the probe but not the pack. A swapped pack would most likely fail rather than pass. Still, `ARCHITECTURE.md:35`'s claim that pack hashes \\\"bind analysis to captures\\\" is not enforced.\\n\\n### 8. Low: native coverage is narrower than the claims\\n- `native.cpp:131` skips C3.w<0. Lines :167–171 test only air, unknown-water and non-water.\\n- So the −2 (projection-rejected) and −1 (underwater, known water, unsolved) fallbacks are **not** natively exercised.\\n- D24 storage is untested.\\n- The straddle check predicts results from the unoccluded draw's point (:153,156). It passed, but it does not show the solve is independent of `depthtex1` in general.\\n\\n\\\"Fallback exact\\\" should name only the three cases tested.\\n\\n### 9. Low (documentation)\\n`ARCHITECTURE.md:25` says the in-range check uses `gl_DepthRange`. The helper hard-codes `0<depth<1` (`glsl:18`). This is harmless under Iris's default range, but reword it.\\n\\n## Scope honesty\\n\\n- No finding above is an implemented fix for source radiance, R2, raster footprint, coverage, headroom, later translucents or hand, or ripples. Those remain open, and the audit's `openGates` says so.\\n- The native result (2617 active, 2611 → 0, 2593 → 0) is fixture-level D32F evidence only.\\n- The old native passivity fixture still has the wrong inverse, so its SSR route1 and exact-color claims stay bounded to that fixture.\\n- Nothing here proves real Iris draw state, visibility or eye origin. That depends on the near/deep captures passing an audit that issue 1 may legitimately fail.\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"029438\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000013646,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":5751,\"output\":\"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 5751)\\nTotal output lines: 301\\n\\n{\\n  \\\"schema\\\": \\\"aqualumen-water-hit-depth-build-v1\\\",\\n  \\\"testOnly\\\": true,\\n  \\\"mode\\\": \\\"baseline\\\",\\n  \\\"observerVersion\\\": \\\"0.1.0-hit1d2\\\",\\n  \\\"inputArchiveSHA256\\\": {\\n    \\\"control\\\": \\\"31540e3de53edec87d73087b1217072a45aee3a04f2b9e2ab7e93d4d6a06231e\\\",\\n    \\\"paired\\\": \\\"9bf027a4905a420a43a6e67eb48155e545a210eb2e654fe7b69fb692e92490a2\\\"\\n  },\\n  \\\"sourcePins\\\": {\\n    \\\"dev/aqualumen/paired/CaptureRules.java\\\": \\\"c88385ca8956981a486ec964760be7ff874ac0327b2af04b3b7eccbafbd8100d\\\",\\n    \\\"dev/aqualumen/paired/GlReadback.java\\\": \\\"1f78b08abdc208e526469b4b98ec79df0506b895377da85f1b1b03ea953136fe\\\",\\n    \\\"dev/aqualumen/paired/Pair…5451 tokens truncated…ly.\\\",\\n    \\\"Baseline retains hit1 shading and implicit raster depth. {"type":"message","status":"completed","id":"msg_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada27d68fc887d0b779a909179767b8","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"output_text","text":"Irisの既定形式は、この3080 Tiでは24-bitの正規化深度として確保されることを確認しました。その組み合わせだけを読み取り側に追加し、再検証を進めています。Claudeが指摘した実行中プロセスの保護と、深度が完全に同値になる場合の判定も見直しました。シェーダー本体は先ほどGPUテストに通ったものと同一です。","annotations":[],"logprobs":[]}],"phase":"commentary","internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792681942.5648346,"content_item_kinds":["unknown"]}}

## 2026-10-22T15:12:29.323Z source line 59135

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a1642b-b88b-74f2-8620-7a2318b26d0d","call_id":"call_420b55bd7690490aa90b63e7700b03a7","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.5 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"42df00\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000015239,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1243,\"output\":\"---\\nname: benchmark-checklist\\ndescription: \\\"Vet a perf measurement (limiter, tuning, limits, errors, repeatability, relevance, and whether the work happened) before you report or act on it. Use when you run a benchmark or report a speedup or regression you measured.\\\"\\n---\\n\\n# Benchmark checklist\\n\\nUse this when you produce a performance number: a PR's before and after, a regression claim, a hillclimb harness, or a library or config choice. Answer each question with evidence from a run, not a guess about the code.\\n\\nFor a quick ballpark the user asked for, one run is enough. Still check questions 4 and 7, and say that it is one run. Skip the rest unless that run looks wrong. A choice between options is never a ballpark.\\n\\n## Before you run anything\\n\\n- Write down the claim you expect to make, in the words you would ship (\\\"export is 30% faster at p50 on the 60k-row dataset\\\"). The questions test that sentence.\\n- Read the measurement script. Note what it times, counts, and ignores.\\n- Check the load average with `uptime` and the core count with `nproc`. If the machine is busy, find out what is running. If you cannot stop it, interleave the sides so both see the same noise, and say so in the report.\\n\\n## The questions\\n\\n1. **Why not double?** Name the limiter. Profile in a run you do not report, because profilers and tracers slow the work down. Use CPU per process (`top`, `pidstat`), a runtime profiler (`node --cpu-prof`, `py-spy`, `perf`), I/O wait, and syscall counts (`strace -c` on Linux), then map the hot spot to source. Watch the load generator too: if it saturates first, you measured the load generator. If a change did not move the number, the limiter explains why, so find it before you call the change useless.\\n2. **Was it tuned?** Run every side the way production runs it: release builds, production flags and env, batching and transaction settings, connection pools, caches as warm or cold as production sees them, and the same versions and data. If one side runs on defaults, you compared configurations, not implementations. A limiter that is a setting (a commit per row, a debug build, a missing index) means that side is untuned. Tune it and measure again before you pick a winner. If you cannot tune it, do not pick a winner from that run. When the user is choosing what to adopt, narrowing the claim to the code as it ships today does not fix this, because they adopt the option, not today's settings.\\n3. **Did it break limits?** Do the arithmetic. Compare bytes per second with disk and network bandwidth, operations per second times cost per operation with your cores, and the time saved with the time the changed piece took. Removing a piece that takes 10% of the run can make the run at most about 11% faster. A result past a limit measured something other than the work, such as a cache, a no-op, or a bug.\\n4. **Did it error?** Count failures and non-success responses, and check that outputs are correct, not just present. Rejections are often fast, and timeouts and retries are slow. If the script does not count errors, add the count.\\n5. **Does it reproduce?** Run each side at least 5 times, alternating (A, B, A, B, and so on) so warmup, lazy initialization, caches, and drift favor neither. Report the median and range. A gap smaller than the run-to-run variation is no measurable difference. When the call is close, use a rank-sum test or the harness's own statistics.\\n6. **Does it matter?** Next to any micro result, measure the end-to-end path a user waits on, with realistic data sizes and concurrency, and report the micro result as a share of the whole. A helper that takes 1% of a request can make the request at most 1% faster, however fast the helper gets.\\n7. **Did it even happen?** Confirm the work ran inside the timed region: the request reached the server, the rows were written, the bytes were read, and the code used the result. Lazy code (generators nobody iterates, promises nobody awaits, results the JIT can discard) and timeouts produce numbers for work that never happened.\\n\\n## Report\\n\\n- Lead with the verdict: faster, slower, no measurable difference, or inconclusive.\\n- Give the number with its unit, the run count, the range, and the limiter. For example, \\\"p50 41 ms → 33 ms, median of 7 runs per side, range 32 to 35 ms after, bound by JSON parsing on one core.\\\"\\n- Call the verdict inconclusive when you claim a difference but cannot name the limiter, when a side ran untuned, or when you could not check questions 4 and 7. Name the gap.\\n- Keep a PR body to one primary number, per the **Opening a PR** playbook. Put the runs, range, and limiter evidence in a linked artifact or notes file.\\n\\n## Other perf material\\n\\n- Vet the **Perf issue** playbook's baseline before the playbook plans from it, and every number after that.\\n- Vet the **Hillclimb** playbook's harness before it is frozen. The frozen harness then prints error and work counts, so each keep-or-revert checks questions 4 and 7 for free.\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"221c58\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000017613,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":72,\"output\":\"{\\\"state\\\":\\\"complete\\\",\\\"run_id\\\":\\\"paired-water-underwater-up-near-depth-baseline-obs2-20261022\\\",\\\"request_id\\\":\\\"76f8d4d9-027f-478b-a1ad-a776ed041721\\\",\\\"scenario\\\":\\\"\\\",\\\"note\\\":\\\"All requested natural-world scenes, raw timings and screenshots recorded\\\",\\\"updated_utc\\\":\\\"2026-10-22T15:12:17.190951425Z\\\"}\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"3132b0\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.069506337,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":10,\"output\":\"2d05d8bd-b869-4f1b-b9de-50a99cab8672\\n\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792681949.3233924}}

{"type":"agent_message","id":"amsg_01a16434-73ee-7131-9315-579ed4a1a07c","author":"/root/depth_ownership_design","recipient":"/root","content":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Message Type: FINAL_ANSWER\nTask name: /root\nSender: /root/depth_ownership_design\nPayload:\nChoose A, with one naming constraint: the marker means **latest known nonwater diagnostic writer**, not universally “depth owner” or “beauty owner.”\n\nThe read sources support this small change. Both `gbuffers_textured.fsh` and shared `terrain.fsh.glsl` discard before writing, currently output only C0/C5, and have no later early-return paths. Their C0 formulas and depth behavior can remain untouched.\n\nTwo alternatives:\n\n- **A — explicit replacement marker.** Extend resolved nonwater program outputs to `RENDERTARGETS:0,5,12,3`. After existing discard decisions, write `gl_FragData[2]=vec4(0)` and `[3]=vec4(0,0,0,-3)`. Disable blending for C12/C3 separately on every affected program. Water retains its existing writes; composite still observes actual distance/Beer in C2.\n- **B — depth-token matching.** Preserve stale water records and compare an owner window-depth token against surviving depth. This can distinguish some depth-changing later draws, but cannot reliably identify alpha-blended draws with depth writes disabled. Depth quantization, equal-depth draws and disagreements in the quantity under test also make it an inference rather than an independent witness.\n\nA provides an affirmative observation independent of the failing distance comparison; B does not meet that goal.\n\nCaller/data contract:\n\n```text\nWater writer:\n  existing C12 water record and C3 optical-point/depth record\n\nKnown nonwater writer surviving discard and depth/stencil:\n  C12 = (0,0,0,0)\n  C3  = (0,0,0,-3)\n\nAudit:\n  exact dual signature -> knownNonwaterDiagnosticWriter\n  existing water signature -> retain strict water depth/distance checks\n  inconsistent signature -> fail closed\n```\n\nWith depth-mask **false**, a half-transparent particle can replace C0 partially while water remains the hardware-depth source. A correctly marks that later nonwater participation, but must not describe it as particle depth ownership. Report depth-mask-independent marker coverage explicitly, with raw counts and component geometry. Marked pixels remain reported, not silently discarded as failed water samples.\n\nMain risks and safeguards:\n\n1. **Blending:** program-wide alpha blending must not attenuate the zero/-3 records. Require per-target overrides for both attachments and actual zero/-3 raw values.\n2. **Coverage:** apply markers only after original discard behavior. Rejected fragments must preserve existing water records.\n3. **Program completeness:** derive the exact affected program list from the pinned archives, including resolved include/fallback/world variants. Do not claim coverage from the shared file name alone.\n4. **Clear/flip behavior:** retain existing allocation, clear values and flipping settings. Verify FINAL reads the same texture side written by the relevant geometry draws; composite must not erase C3/C12.\n5. **Scope:** hand, particles or other uninstrumented paths remain unknown; no assertion of complete final-beauty provenance.\n\nMinimal verification:\n\n- Native fail-first: original later overlay leaves stale water records; instrumented overlay produces the exact dual marker.\n- Alpha 0.5 with depth-mask true and false: C0/depth are byte-identical to their corresponding controls; marker behavior matches surviving fragments.\n- Discarded/occluded overlays leave water records unchanged; later water rewrites its own records.\n- Actual deep capture: report marker count/components, retain the original 445-pixel failure Evidence, and fail on **every remaining water-classified** depth/distance violation.\n- New baseline3/candidate3 receipts pin program list, marker protocol and observer versions. Require unchanged control ZIPs and byte-identical water/VS/composite/physics sources relative to pinned generation2.\n\nThis is a safe observer-only ownership correction under those contracts—not a physical or visual fix."}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792682521.5828865}}

## 2026-10-22T15:22:34.952Z source line 59299

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16434-f648-7580-b916-1d7261e354a8","call_id":"call_af7cd822dbf5432bb13608cc9a69cad1","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 1.9 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"bfe00b\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.00001588,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":1377,\"output\":\"I did 6 reads: `gbuffers_textured.fsh`, `lib/terrain.fsh.glsl`, two reads of `tools/water_depth_candidate.py`, a grep of `shaders.properties`, and a glob of the `gbuffers_*` files. I wrote nothing, and that includes the plan file, because you said no writes.\\n\\n## Who writes what, and data shape\\n\\n- **C0 and C5:** `gbuffers_textured.fsh:5` and `lib/terrain.fsh.glsl:10` both declare `DRAWBUFFERS:05`. The terrain include is also used by terrain, textured_lit, entities, hand, particles and weather.\\n- **C12 and C3:** only water writes these. C3 is `(opticalPoint, projectedDepth | -1 | -2)` (`water_depth_candidate.py:62-63`).\\n- **C2:** composite overwrites it with distance and Beer transmission (`:73-74`).\\n- **Depth:** `depthtex1` is the depth before translucents. So `depth1=1` with `depth0` closer than water means a translucent-phase draw that ran after water won that pixel. Square components fit camera-facing particle sprites, but that is still inference. Your requirement for a witness stands.\\n- **Blend:** the only blend key is `blend.gbuffers_water=SRC_ALPHA ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA ONE ONE_MINUS_SRC_ALPHA` (`shaders.properties:17`). It applies to every water output, C3 included. That means C3.w (the depth) acts as source alpha. Before relying on exact C3 values, confirm that the generated pack overrides this per buffer. I couldn't check it within the read budget.\\n\\n## Choice: A is safe, with conditions\\n\\nA records who last wrote each pixel. B classifies pixels using the depth it is supposed to be testing, which is circular. B also needs a channel that doesn't exist (C3.w is taken), and it can't name an owner. With alpha-blended draws that don't write depth, a depth token can't separate \\\"touched\\\" from \\\"changed\\\". I'd reject B.\\n\\nUnder A, a final −3 means the last fragment to survive discard at that pixel came from a nonwater program. Call it `lastWriterNonwater`, not \\\"later\\\". The terrain include also runs for opaque terrain, which draws before water. Wherever water later wins, it overwrites the marker, so last writer is the right meaning.\\n\\n## Specific risks\\n\\n1. **Blend can make the marker disappear.** Translucent passes (particles, clouds, weather) use Iris default alpha blend. Writing `C12=vec4(0)` with alpha 0 leaves the stale value in place. Writing C3.w=−3 with alpha 0.5 produces a mixed value. You need `blend.<program>.colortex12=off` and `.colortex3=off`. With Iris fallbacks (particles/entities/hand/weather → textured_lit; clouds/beacon/spidereyes/glint/skytextured → textured), it's unclear whether those keys bind to the fallback source name or to the original program ID. Witness this natively; don't assume it.\\n2. **Coverage loss.** A pixel with a depth-mask-false particle over a genuinely wrong water depth gets marked, and its water record is erased. That would hide a real failure. Fix: baseline3/candidate3 come from pinned run-2 packs, the scene is frozen, and the water bytes are identical, so join run 3 against run 2 per pixel:\\n   - Every marked pixel must have `depth0_3` strictly closer than run 2's projected water depth.\\n   - Otherwise it counts as a failure.\\n3. **Extra −3 pixels.** Terrain, sun/moon (skytextured → textured) and clouds now leave −3 where C3 used to be cleared to 0. Declare this in the protocol and update any C3 readers that treat w==0 as \\\"none\\\".\\n4. **Unlisted paths.** `gbuffers_basic` (block outline, leash, lines) draws after translucents. Either patch it or list it as unmarked in `programList`. Leave `skybasic` alone. Ice and stained glass go through `gbuffers_water`, so they still produce water records.\\n5. **Discard order.** The marker must be written after `discard`, so cut-out texels keep the water record.\\n6. **Output formats.** Confirm C12 and C3 formats accept exact 0 and −3 (C3 should be float).\\n\\n## Minimal contracts\\n\\n- Patch only C12 and C3 outputs: `RENDERTARGETS:0,5,12,3` with `C12=vec4(0)` and `C3=vec4(0,0,0,-3)`.\\n- Assert C0, C5 and the depth expressions are byte-identical to before.\\n- Water, VS, composite, physics and control ZIPs stay byte-identical. Verify this from the pack hash table in the receipt.\\n- New versions are hit1d3/depth3, with a new output directory (`exist_ok=False`, as now), so the 445 report is untouched.\\n- Record in the manifest: `markerProtocol`, `programList` (patched, fallback-covered, explicitly unmarked), `laterNonwaterMarker:-3`, and blend keys.\\n\\n## Tests\\n\\n**Native, fail-first:** draw a water quad, then a particle quad over it, and run 6 checks:\\n1. **Before the patch:** C12 and C3 equal the water values (stale). This must fail the ownership assertion.\\n2. **After the patch:** C12 is exactly 0 and C3 is bit-exactly (0,0,0,−3).\\n3. **Passivity:** C0 and depth are bit-identical to the unpatched run at alpha 0.5, with depth mask true and depth mask false.\\n4. **Blend-on negative control:** C12 stays stale or mixed, which proves the off key is what does the work.\\n5. **Discard:** a texel with alpha < 0.1 keeps the water record.\\n6. **Water after a particle with depth mask false:** water takes the pixel back.\\n\\n**Actual deep run:**\\n- Report the marker count and connected components.\\n- All 445 failing pixels must be marked.\\n- Unmarked mismatches must be zero, against the same 1 mm budget.\\n- Every marked pixel must pass the run-2 join (strictly closer).\\n- Every unmarked pixel's `depth0` must be bitwise equal to run 2.\\n\\nPassing these shows who owns each pixel and nothing more. Visibility, particle physics and visual acceptance remain separate gates.\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"1fc393\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.000010309,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":9,\"output\":\"/tmp/aqualumen-owner-writer.R4Du2Q\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"9c3a0c\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.435023075,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":16,\"output\":\"Archived one same-FINAL paired capture and raw scene Evidence\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"27279a\",\"wall_time_seconds\":1.001670052,\"session_id\":27474,\"original_token_count\":0,\"output\":\"\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792682554.9524782}}

[Task 3]\\n- Function-relative diff hunks may need offset/fuzz when applying; no patch in this task was applied or tested despite the success of the proposed convergence logic. [Task 4]\\n---\\nname: principle-boundary-discipline\\ndescription: \\\"Apply when wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Concentrate guards at system boundaries (CLI, config, network, external APIs); trust internal types and keep business logic in pure functions.\\\"\\nuser-invocable: false\\n---\\n\\n# Boundary Discipline\\n\\nPlace validation, type narrowing, and error handling at system boundaries. Trust internal code unconditionally. Business logic lives in pure functions. The shell is thin and mechanical.\\n\\n**Why:** Scattered validation is noisy, redundant, and gives a false sense of safety. Keep logic out of framework wiring so it can be tested without the framework.\\n\\n**The pattern:**\\n- **At boundaries** (CLI args, config files, external APIs, network protocols): validate, return errors, handle defensively.\\n- **Inside the system:** typed data, error propagation, no re-validation. Trust the types.\\n- **Across the boundary.** Expose domain concepts, not the boundary's private representation. Keep general-purpose mechanism inside and special-purpose policy at the edge.\\n\\n**Applications:**\\n\\nValidation and error handling:\\n- Validate config at parse time (the boundary), not inside business logic\\n- Parse raw data into domain types at the boundary\\n- Do not re-export transport, storage, framework, or wire types through the public surface\\n- No redundant nil checks deep in call chains if the boundary already validated\\n\\nCode organization:\\n- Business logic in pure functions with no framework dependencies\\n- Parse functions: pure transforms from raw bytes to typed state\\n- Prompt construction: structured state in, string out\\n- Scoring and assessment: pure transforms from state to results\\n\\n**The tests:**\\n- \\\"Is this data crossing a system boundary right now?\\\" If not, validation is redundant.\\n- \\\"Can this be a pure function that the shell just calls?\\\" If yes, extract it.\\n---\\nname: principle-build-the-lever\\ndescription: \\\"Apply to any non-trivial work, not just bulk work: edits, migrations, analyses, checks. Build the tool that does it or proves it (codemod, script, generator, or a skill your subagents follow) instead of working by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer can rerun.\\\"\\nuser-invocable: false\\n---\\n# Build the Lever\\n\\nWhen the work isn't trivial, build the tool that does it instead of doing it by hand.\\n\\nA codemod, generator, or script does the work the same way every time and reruns for free. The tool is one artifact a reviewer can read and rerun to check the work. Hand-done changes can only be re-verified by redoing them. A deterministic script turns \\\"trust me\\\" into \\\"run this\\\".\\n\\nDefault to building the lever. Skip it only when the task is trivial, a couple of obvious edits you can see at a glance.\\n\\n- Do the first unit by hand to learn the recipe, then build the tool. Prove it by rerunning it on that unit and diffing against your hand-done version. Make the lever safe to rerun.\\n- For a comparison tool, seed one known-bad change for each output it claims to compare, and confirm the tool reports each mismatch. For example, change a total, then separately swap item assignments while keeping the totals. If required input is missing, the tool must refuse or report the result as inconclusive, never as a match. Keep these controls with the experiment and rerun them before you trust its results.\\n- Codemod or script for edits, generator for repetitive files, a dump-to-sqlite query for analysis, a rerunnable check for verification.\\n- A deterministic lever beats fan-out. If the tool can process every unit in one pass, run it yourself. Don't fan out delegates to hand-apply what a script can do.\\n- When you fan work out to subagents, write the lever as a skill they all read: the recipe, the verification contract, and the do-not-touch fences in one artifact. Keep it outside the delegates' write scope so they can't quietly edit the contract.\\n- Applying this principle produces a file. If you cited it and there is no codemod, script, generator, or delegate skill in the diff, you didn't apply it.\\n- Commit the lever when the work outlives the session.\\n\\nThe bar is triviality, not repetition. A one-off still earns a lever when the lever is what makes the work checkable. Per the [Laziness Protocol](../principle-laziness-protocol/SKILL.md), build the smallest script that does or proves the job, never a framework.\\n\\nDistinct from [Encode Lessons in Structure](../principle-encode-lessons-in-structure/SKILL.md), which makes a recurring instruction a durable guardrail. This is throughput and reviewability on the work in front of you. For scripting the verification itself, see [Prove It Works](../principle-prove-it-works/SKILL.md).\\n\"}"},{"type":"input_text","text":"{\"chunk_id\":\"1c9b0b\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.069795392,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":82,\"output\":\"{\\n  \\\"request_id\\\": \\\"ba231fcf-5fc1-4b24-9174-9f076c83de48\\\",\\n  \\\"action\\\": \\\"start\\\",\\n  \\\"world_id\\\": \\\"b22dbccb-2970-41e6-847f-f183b1ec923d\\\",\\n  \\\"run_id\\\": \\\"paired-water-underwater-up-deep-depth-baseline-obs3-20261022\\\",\\n  \\\"scenarios\\\": \\\"underwater-up-deep\\\",\\n  \\\"warmup_seconds\\\": \\\"10.0\\\",\\n  \\\"sample_seconds\\\": \\\"20.0\\\",\\n  \\\"auto_quit\\\": \\\"false\\\"\\n}\\n\"}"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792683322.7703404}}

## 2026-10-22T15:50:33.535Z source line 59649

{"type":"custom_tool_call","id":"ctc_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada3052e01c87d0a7ca2d0cfc6b0cc3","status":"completed","call_id":"call_e01c507656644a1b8fede3bfc0d0c4d3","name":"exec","input":"text(await tools.apply_patch(\"*** Begin Patch\\n*** Add File: /home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen/reports/2026-10-22-water-hit-depth/SUMMARY.md\\n+# 水面の深度と水中減衰を統一するTEST\\n+\\n+連続水面を使う前回のhit1候補に残っていた、光学計算の交点と深度・水中減衰距離の不一致を修正しました。実RTX 3080 Ti、Minecraft 1.21.1／Iris 1.8.8、1600×900の隔離TESTで確認しました。魚眼状の湾曲や折返し全体を解消したという結果ではありません。実画像にも残っています。\\n+\\n+Codexが実装・GPU検証・Minecraft操作を担当しました。実Claude CodeのOpusが独立設計、設計の再検討、コードレビューと診断用データの設計を担当しました。Claudeの呼び出しはread-onlyで、Claudeが実装したとは扱っていません。応答、モデル、セッションID、終了コードは各`claude-*`に保存しています。\\n+\\n+## 確認した結果\\n+\\n+| 記録 | 検査した水面画素 | 減衰距離と光学交点距離の最大差 |\\n+|---|---:|---:|\\n+| 修正前・近距離 | 1,440,000 | 約57.68 mm |\\n+| 修正後・近距離 | 1,440,000 | 約0.00134 mm |\\n+| 修正前・深い位置、診断値で水面と識別 | 1,439,567 | 約75.96 mm |\\n+| 修正後・深い位置、診断値で水面と識別 | 1,439,370 | 約0.05022 mm |\\n+\\n+修正後は、対象水面の深度・距離・Beer透過率の違反がすべて0です。事前に宣言した距離1 mm、D24深度1量子、透過率絶対差1e-6の基準を変更していません。最遠の対象交点は約5.50 mで、遠距離一般の精度を証明する検査ではありません。\\n+\\n+近距離はgeneration2の記録、深い位置はgeneration3の明示的な描画診断付き記録です。generation3は非水面の診断出力だけを追加しています。水面・頂点・composite・物理モデルとcontrol ZIPはgeneration2から変更していません。完了済みの近距離Minecraft撮影は再実行せず、保存データを最終監査コードで再検査しました。\\n+\\n+法線、光学交点、視線は比較可能な水面で完全一致しました。深い位置の両記録は1,438,937画素で相互比較できます。C1のラスタ位置、視線C14、opaque depthtex1はフレーム全体でも同一でした。物理モデルや露出を変えて輪郭を隠す変更ではありません。\\n+\\n+## 非水面の描画を、水面の失敗として数えていた問題\\n+\\n+初期の深い位置では修正前445画素、修正後426画素が厳密検査に失敗しました。どちらも保存しています。誤差は数mに達し、深度精度では説明できません。小さな四角い描画箇所で、水面の記録だけが古く残っていました。\\n+\\n+そのため、既知の非水面プログラムが描画した画素には、C12のゼロとC3の(0,0,0,-3)を同時に書く診断を追加しました。距離が合わない画素を条件で除外する方法ではありません。修正前の新記録で433画素、修正後で630画素に正確な診断値を観測しました。残った水面の違反は0です。\\n+\\n+この値は「最後に既知の非水面プログラムが書いた」という意味です。深度の所有者や最終画像の全寄与を意味しません。深度を書かない半透明描画も診断値を残すことをGPUで確認しています。粒子等の時間は固定していないため、過去445画素との座標一致は要求していません。未対応の描画経路は残っています。\\n+\\n+## 実装と検証\\n+\\n+受理した交点を`gl_FragDepth`に投影し、compositeが同じ水面までの距離を復元します。初期トレース距離と影の評価点も交点に合わせました。非水面や未受理の経路ではラスタ深度に戻ります。太陽光の入射面高さなど、別の近似はこの変更では直していません。\\n+\\n+実NVIDIAのネイティブ検査では、旧方式の2,611画素の深度不一致と、遮蔽物を挟む両方向の2,593画素の前後関係誤りが、補正後は0になりました。完全遮蔽、air・unknown-water・nonwaterの一致、8MRTの水面C0／深度一致を確認しました。これはそのGPU検証器の結果であり、Minecraft内の遮蔽物検査ではありません。\\n+\\n+非水面の診断はunlit／litの2系統で、深度書込あり・なしの両方を検査しました。各289画素に診断値が現れ、C0と深度は診断なしとバイト一致しました。ブレンドを有効にした誤設定、discard、遮蔽、合成した水面レコードによる再描画も検査しています。最後の再描画はプロトコル用の合成シェーダーであり、実水面プログラムの包括的検査ではありません。\\n+\\n+58件のテストが成功しました。保存された実機記録に距離・透過率・深度・交点・opaque同値・誤ったpack hashを個別に注入すると検査が失敗することも確認しました。\\n+\\n+途中で検証器の逆射影行列の初期値不足と、Irisのunsized 24-bit深度形式への未対応を発見しました。旧失敗を保存し、新しい検証器では行列積を検査、読み取り側では実形式・ビット数・型を記録しています。RGBA32Fの色検査を緩めた変更ではありません。\\n+\\n+## 性能と通常設定\\n+\\n+このTESTは非常に重く、通常プレイ向けではありません。修正後の単発記録は近距離約1.90 FPS、深い位置約2.36 FPSでした。p50は約453／409 msです。1600×900の計測用構成であり、通常0.6や目標2560×1440のFPSとしては扱いません。繰り返し交互計測と律速のプロファイルがないため、高速化・低速化の判定は保留です。生データは`TIMING-OBSERVATIONS.json`です。\\n+\\n+Labは通常のAqualumen 0.6／physics 0.2に戻しました。config・Mods・instance・通常ワールドのハッシュが一致し、通常ワールドの巻き戻しはしていません。最終generation3のlauncher終了コードは両方0、Lab Javaなし、TEST操作マーカーなしを確認しています。過去generation2 candidateの外側ラッパー143は別記録に残し、終了コード0とは扱っていません。\\n+\\n+通常の`shaders/`と既存の未コミット変更は保持しました。今回の候補は固定17秒・相互作用リップル0・既知の深い水・限定footprint専用で、通常版に導入していません。\\n+\\n+## 残る検証\\n+\\n+湾曲した輪郭全体の見た目、二次水面・R2、画素内の可視性、メッシュ外の被覆、天井との接触、動く波、一般ワールド、実Minecraftでの遮蔽物との前後関係、影と光源側の放射輸送、通常プレイ性能は未完了です。次の実装単位は交点計算の軽量化と、その後の可視性検査です。このTESTを通常版として出荷する段階ではありません。\\n+\\n+Model the
