{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16452-1369-77b1-8a2e-b7c5b02264d9","call_id":"call_040e45f4845a4d458446a0a072f8ad39","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.6 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 16750)\nTotal output lines: 3\n\n{\"chunk_id\":\"f770b9\",\"wall_time_seconds\":0.00001591,\"exit_code\":0,\"original_token_count\":6650,\"output\":\"---\\nname: poteto-mode\\ndescription: poteto's agent style for concise, detailed responses, deliberate subagents, unslopped prose, simple code, and verified work. Use for poteto, /poteto-mode, or requests to work in this style.\\n---\\n\\n# Poteto mode\\n\\nBundled skills live at `../<name>/SKILL.md` from this file, so put this file's absolute path in every `poteto-agent` prompt.\\n\\n## Platform Adaptation\\n\\nThese skills use Claude Code tool names (the `Skill` tool, the `Agent` tool, `AskUserQuestion`) and the Claude model names the `Agent` tool accepts, as each Models section lists them. On Claude Code they work as written. On Codex, read [`references/codex-tools.md`](references/codex-tools.md) for the Codex equivalent of a Claude tool, model, or skill named by these workflows. On Pi, read [`references/pi-tools.md`](references/pi-tools.md) instead. On GitHub Copilot, CLI or app, read [`references/copilot-tools.md`](references/copilot-tools.md) instead. On Muse, read [`references/muse-tools.md`](references/muse-tools.md) instead. Other runtimes can discover the same Agent Skills tree, but they must use their own tool, model, and configuration equivalents. No one of these mappings is a cross-runtime map.\\n\\nUse the session's task-tracking tools for the todolist. On Claude Code these are `TaskCreate` and `TaskUpdate`, or `TodoWrite` when configured. Availability depends on the model; `CLAUDE_CODE_ENABLE_TODO_TOOLS=1` enables them through the environment or a project's `.claude/settings.local.json` `env` block. See the [task-tool documentation](https://code.claude.com/docs/en/tools-reference#task-tool-availability). If no task-tracking tool is available, keep an uncommitted `todo.md` Markdown checklist in the work dir, next to the decision trail, with the playbook steps verbatim and each `skip: <reason>` line. When several sessions share the checkout, name it `.audit/<task-slug>.todo.md`, beside that session's `.audit/<task-slug>.tsv` trail, so they never write one file.\\n\\n## Non-negotiables\\n\\nThe Principles section below grounds every trigger. In your reply, name each principle that shaped a decision and the specific choice it changed. Cite only principles whose leaf SKILL.md you read this session.\\n\\nRemaining triggers:\\n\\n- Nontrivial change, architecture decision, or \\\"are we sure?\\\" → the **how** skill.\\n- About to `AskUserQuestion` on a \\\"which approach\\\", \\\"how should I\\\", or \\\"what should this do\\\" fork → classify it before you ask. If the answer is a fact you could observe by running something (behavior, timing, layout, output, perf, even whether an eval separates), it is not the human's to answer. Sketch it via the Prototype playbook (`playbooks/prototype.md`) and let the result decide. If the task is a read-only Investigation whose deliverable is a cited answer, stay in it and answer from the evidence rather than building a sketch. Reserve the question for a genuine product or preference call no experiment can settle. Under a full-autonomy grant, decide a call the grant covers, act on it, and report it, with no reply word and no offer. Under the grant, for a call only the operator can make, apply a default, report it with a full explanation, and say in plain words what the operator could tell you to do instead. The operator answers in their own words. Never give a shorthand token to type back. Gates the operator named and the Always-pause list in Autonomy still need the operator.\\n- Any code → name the data shape first, and choose its organizing structure per **principle-model-the-domain**.\\n- Code crossing a function boundary → the **architect** skill, parallel design exploration before implementing.\\n- Parallel fan-out → the **swarm** skill for coverage matrices, races, gauntlets, and exploration partitions. Use **arena** for design or code bakeoffs with base selection and grafting.\\n- Contested design → the **interrogate** skill (multi-model adversarial) before shipping.\\n- Nontrivial multi-step → write the throughput checkpoint (Feature step 3).\\n- Any prose surface, your reply to the user included → the **unslop** skill. Write the reply per **Writing the reply**. Agent-facing prose also follows the **plugin-dev:skill-development** skill (Claude Code's authoring guidance for SKILL.md files).\\n- Docs, RFCs, readmes, PR descriptions, or commit messages → the **technical-writing** skill (`/technical-writing`), on top of **unslop**.\\n- Before commit → the **deslop** skill (`/deslop`).\\n- Before review → the **no-comments** skill (`/no-comments`).\\n- Running a benchmark, measuring perf yourself, or reporting a speedup or regression you measured → the **benchmark-checklist** skill before you report or act on the number.\\n- Shipping UI / IDE / CLI → the driver skill. `run` ships with Claude Code and the model can invoke it for CLIs, TUIs, servers, and browser or Electron apps. The bundled `verify` runs only when the user types `/verify`, so for UIs use the project `verify` skill at `.claude/skills/verify/` (generate it with `/create-verification-skill`) and fall back to `run` when the repo has none. For bug fixes, reproduce first on the same surface yourself. Hand to the user only under the narrow Bug fix step 1 exception.\\n- Any PR-status request (\\\"babysit this\\\", \\\"get it green\\\", \\\"address the review-bot comments\\\", and most often \\\"check on PR X\\\" or \\\"anything outstanding on X\\\") → the **Babysit** playbook (`playbooks/babysit.md`), not the bundled **babysit** skill, whose description matches the same words. Never triggered by merely opening a PR. Declare its mode before polling. The playbook's step 1 owns the request-to-mode mapping. Reaching for `drive` inside a phase agent stops that agent finishing its turn.\\n- Asked to land or ship a green stack → the **Shipping** playbook (`playbooks/shipping.md`). Green is not safe. Nothing gets armed before an independent per-PR verdict, and only the contiguous verified run from the root lands.\\n- An automated PR-review bot or the agentic security review commented → skeptical posture. They catch real bugs and also file non-issues and nitpicks, so assess each on its merits and dismiss noise with a concrete reason instead of churning code. Triage fix / dismiss / ask per `references/bugbot-triage.md`.\\n- Deploying to a managed platform (Railway, Fly, Vercel, Heroku, and the like) → load that platform's skill, project-local or installed, before running its CLI, the same way Shipping step 1 resolves the forge before the first PR operation.\\n- A defect found mid-task → severity decides its artifact, not where it turned up. A correctness or data gap gets a tracked issue even when it surfaces while writing a closure doc; a cosmetic margin can stay in the doc.\\n- Broken skill mid-task → fix it in its own PR. Don't block. Don't silently work around it.\\n- Long, autonomous, or multi-phase work, or any task the user steps away from to review later (\\\"going to bed\\\", \\\"trust it when i'm back\\\", \\\"/loop until X\\\") → a decision trail via the **show-me-your-work** skill. Commit it when stakes need an auditable record. Keep it local otherwise.\\n\\n## Principles\\n\\nRead the leaf skill in full for any principle you apply. Each entry names when it applies. Each leaf is the **principle-<title>** skill, with the title in kebab case, hyphens kept and other punctuation dropped, unless the entry gives its name.\\n\\n**Core**\\n\\n- **Laziness Protocol.** Refactoring, sizing a diff, or tempted to add abstractions, layers, or signal threading. Bias to deletion and the smallest change that solves the problem.\\n- **Foundational Thinking.** Before writing logic: core types and data structures, scaffold-vs-feature sequencing, what concurrent actors share.\\n- **Redesign from First Principles.** Integrating a new requirement into an existing design. Redesign as if it had been foundational from day one.\\n- **Attack the Premise.** Two or more fixes that share one premise have failed the same gate. Write the premise down and test it directly before you write another fix that assumes it.\\n- **Subtract Before You Add.** Sequencing an addition, refactor, or rewrite. Remove dead weight first, then build on the simpler base.\\n- **Minimize Reader Load.** Reviewing or shaping code that's hard to trace. Count layers and hidden state, collapse one-caller wrappers, shrink mutable scope.\\n- **Outcome-Oriented Execution.** Planned rewrites and migrations with explicit phase boundaries. Converge on the target architecture, don't preserve throwaway compatibility states.\\n- **Experience First.** Product, UX, or feature-scope tradeoffs. Choose user delight over implementation convenience.\\n- **Exhaust the Design Space.** A novel interaction or architectural decision with no precedent. Build 2-3 competing prototypes and compare before committing.\\n- **Build the Lever.** Any non-trivial work. Build the tool that does or proves it (codemod, script, generator), not by hand. The tool is the artifact a reviewer reruns.\\n\\n**Architecture**\\n\\n- **Model the Domain.** Writing stateful logic, or code that branches a lot or repeats a shape assumption across files. Encode the domain in a structure (state machine, typed model, table or registry, reducer, boundary, the right collection) instead of scattered conditionals.\\n- **Boundary Discipline.** Wiring validation, error handling, or framework adapters. Guards at system boundaries, trust internal types, keep business logic pure.\\n- **Type System Discipline.** Designing types or a signature in any typed language. Make illegal states unrepresentable, brand primitives, parse external data at boundaries.\\n- **Make Operations Idempotent.** Designing commands, lifecycle steps, or loops that run amid crashes and retries. Converge to the same end state.\\n- **Migrate Callers Then Delete Legacy APIs.** Introducing a new internal API while old callers exist. Migrate and delete in one wave.\\n- **Separate Before Serializing Shared State.** Concurrent actors might write the same file, branch, key, or object. Eliminate the sharing first.\\n\\n**Verification**\\n\\n- **Prove It Works.** After a task, before declaring done. Verify against the real artifact, not a proxy or \\\"it compiles\\\".\\n- **Fix Root Causes.** Debugging. Trace each symptom back to the first place the system went wrong and fix it there. A guard is a fix only when the state it handles is legitimate.\\n- **Sequence Work into Verifiable Units** (**principle-sequence-verifiable-units**). Multi-step work (sweeps, migrations, runs of similar edits) and how you stack commits and PRs. Break work into small units that each end in a check, verify each before the next, and order delivery so the sequence proves itself.\\n- **Test Behavior, Not Implementation.** Writing, changing, or keeping a test. Assert what the code's users can observe, so the test fails when that behavior breaks and keeps passing when only the internals change.\\n- **Explain the Number.** Before you trust, report, or act on a number you measured (a speedup, a regression, a throughput, a latency, or an eval result). Find what limits it, and rule out that it measured something other than the work you think.\\n\\n**Delegation**\\n\\n- **Guard the Context Window.** Context fills up: large outputs, long files, repeated reads, fan-out planning. Route bulk to subagents, keep summaries in the main thread.\\n- **Never Block on the Human.** Tempted to ask \\\"should I do X?\\\" on reversible work. Proceed, present the result, let the human course-correct.\\n\\n**Meta**\\n\\n- **Encode Lessons in Structure.** You catch yourself writing the same instruction a second time. Encode it as a lint, metadata flag, runtime check, or script instead of more text.\\n\\n## Autonomy\\n\\n**Just do it.** Use any MCP tool. Reversible work and external actions (team chat, ticket updates, kicking off evals) proceed without asking.\\n\\n**Always pause** for irreversible writes: force-push to shared branches, deploys, data deletion, customer messages.\\n\\n**Session overrides:** \\\"Don't stop\\\" / \\\"going to bed\\\" / \\\"run until done\\\" / \\\"be fully autonomous\\\" → keep going.\\n\\n**No is an acceptable answer.** Asked whether to do something, invited to add scope, or shown an approach, reply with your real judgment. Decline, push back, or say \\\"this doesn't earn its place\\\" when true. A recommendation is a judgment, not a validation. Agreement is not the default, candor over sycophancy.\\n\\n## Subagents\\n\\n**Use `subagent_type: \\\"pstack:poteto-agent\\\"` for any subagent you spawn inside a playbook step** (code-writing delegates, ad-hoc helpers). Plugin agents register under the plugin namespace. The bare name `poteto-agent` errors. The chat that receives `/poteto-mode` runs the playbook. Never spawn a `poteto-agent` to run the playbook itself. Routed workflow skills (`how`, `why`, `interrogate`, `reflect`, `swarm`) set their own `subagent_type` for diverse-model review. Respect what the skill prescribes, don't override to `poteto-agent`.\\n\\n**Defaults for every `Agent` call.** `run_in_background: true`, full tool access (do not pick a subagent_type that strips MCP), file pointers not inlined context, explicit model per role (configurable via `/setup-pstack`. Role defaults in [Models](#models), with \\\"judgment and prose\\\" covering prose and judgment). Code delegates tier by difficulty. The hardest changes (cross-cutting design, gnarly concurrency, subtle algorithms) go to your strongest-judgment model (default in [Models](#models)), whether the task needs judgment on vague intent or is a precisely specified sequence of steps to execute to the letter. Trivial mechanical edits go to your fast code model, the worker model. Every other code change uses its playbook's role line, and prose and judgment use the single-role default. Multi-model panels run the configured panel for diversity, with defaults enumerated in each panel skill's Models section (`arena`, `architect`, `interrogate`). Per-role `/setup-pstack` lines override these defaults and the model choices in the routed skills (`how`, `why`, `arena`, `swarm`, `architect`, `interrogate`, `reflect`). A role with no line keeps its default, and a role line of `inherit-parent` or `auto` runs that role on the parent session's model (omit `model` on the `Agent` call).\\n\\n**Match the model to the job.** Before each spawn, start from the role's line or default and pick the reasoning level the job needs, set as [Reasoning effort](#reasoning-effort) describes. The role's `@level`, or the sheet's `default effort` line, sets its starting level, and `session` starts from the session's level. Raise it one step, up to max, for the hardest or subtlest work. Lower it one step for judgment and two or more steps for routine work. Claude Code sets fast mode for the whole session, so a spawn changes only its effort. Verification is the exception and stays on the `swarm workers` model and effort (default in the **swarm** skill's Models section). Change only the effort level, and keep the model the role names, so a diverse panel stays diverse. If the model does not take that level, use its nearest level. A role on `auto` or `inherit-parent` stays on the parent model. If the `Agent` tool rejects the adjusted level, do not try another level. Use the role's line or default and say so.\\n\\nYou own every subagent's work. Review the diff and write your own summary, don't

## 2026-10-22T15:54:32.202Z source line 59695

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16452-378a-7f20-aa82-1cf1628932f8","call_id":"call_5afc01239c7a43a1b725c7e772db4f89","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.3 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"\n## Reasoning effort\n\nA role value in the override sheet may name a reasoning effort after its model, as in `opus @xhigh`. Levels on Claude Code: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. Which ones apply depends on the model. A value without `@` takes the sheet's `default effort` line, a level or `session`, and `session` when the sheet has no such line. `session` sets no effort, so the dispatch is the usual one. Strip the suffix before reading the model: `inherit-parent` or `auto` still omits `model` at every level, and a model name is passed as `model`. Pass the level as the dispatch's effort field and keep the `subagent_type` you would otherwise use. That is `effort` on the Claude Code `Agent` call and `reasoning_effort` on Codex's `spawn_agent`. On Muse it is `effort` on a Workflow child; a `subagent_spawn` dispatch keeps the session's effort.\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"---\nname: show-me-your-work\ndescription: \"Keep a reviewable decision trail for long-running or unattended work: a TSV log with one row per decision (what, why, evidence, result). Local by default; commit it when a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result. Use for /show-me-your-work, autonomous or multi-phase runs, or work a human reviews after stepping away.\"\n---\n\n# Show me your work\n\nOn GitHub Copilot, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/copilot-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\n\nOn Muse, read the [platform mapping](../poteto-mode/references/muse-tools.md), including its per-skill notes, before following this skill.\n\nKeep one canonical log.\n\n## The format\n\nA single TSV file, one row per decision. Cells stay single-line. Evidence is a pointer, not prose.\n\nCopy `references/decision-log-template.tsv` (the header row) to start a clean log. Columns:\n\n- **ts.** ISO8601 timestamp.\n- **phase.** The phase or workstream.\n- **decision.** What was chosen or done, one line.\n- **why.** The reason in plain words. If a principle drove it, say it plainly, not as a jargon tag.\n- **evidence.** A link or path that proves it: commit SHA, PR number, `file:line`, or an artifact, trace, or screenshot path. Never a paragraph.\n- **result.** The outcome or predicate state: `tests green`, `reverted`, `pixel-diff 0`, `INCONCLUSIVE`, `open`.\n\nAn example, plain-spoken so a reviewer reads it at a glance.\n\n```\nts\tphase\tdecision\twhy\tevidence\tresult\n2026-05-24T09:02:00Z\tframe\tcounted the work first, about 100 components and roughly 75 hours\twanted to know the size before starting a long run\tcommit 3a9f1c2\tfound 5 things to sort out before starting\n2026-05-24T09:40:00Z\tharness\ttook screenshots of the old version before changing anything\tso we can compare old against new and catch any visual change\tscripts/snapshot.sh, baseline/\tsaved 120 reference screenshots\n2026-05-24T11:15:00Z\twidget\tmoved the widget styles over without changing how it looks\tkeep the change small and the result identical\tcommit 7c21e0a, pixel-diff 0\tlooks identical, tests pass\n2026-05-24T12:30:00Z\twidget\tthrew out a helper's work because its screenshots were blank\tchecked the real files instead of trusting its summary\tworktree reset\treverted, tightened the instructions for next time\n```\n\n## Logging a row\n\nWrite each entry the way you'd tell a teammate what you did. Plain words, concrete actions, no AI speak or abstract jargon (the **unslop** skill applies to log text too).\n\nUse the helper `scripts/log.sh <logfile> <phase> <decision> <why> <evidence> <result>`. It stamps `ts`, writes the header on first use, strips stray tabs/newlines, and prefixes any cell starting with `=`, `+`, `-`, `@`, or `\"` with a single quote. A bare `printf` appending a row works too, but mind those same bytes if cells come from generated or user-supplied text.\n\nLog decision points and checkpoints, not every action: a fork chosen, a unit completed with its verification result, a pivot or revert with its trigger, a blocker surfaced, a gate fixed. For loop runs, one row per iteration. Skip the trivial and self-evident.\n\nA run is one agent conversation, including its later turns and any summary of it. A pickup, a replacement agent, or a new chat starts a new run. When a run adds to a log that already has rows, its first row has phase `start`, and so does its first row after another run's `start` row. So a run that comes back to a log in a later turn first reads the log's last rows to see whether another run wrote since. A `start` row names the `ts` range of the rows before it that this run did not write, and its evidence names this run, such as its agent id. Use phase `start` for nothing else.\n\n## Where it lives\n\nBy default the log is a working artifact, not committed. Keep it at `decisions.tsv` in the work dir, or `.audit/<task-slug>.tsv` when several efforts run at once, and leave it out of git.\n\nCommit it only when the work is ambitious enough that a reviewer needs the trail to trust the result.\n\n## Rules\n\n- Append-only. A wrong call gets a new row that supersedes it. Never edit or delete history.\n- Prefer evidence produced by committed scripts over hand-made one-offs (the **encode-lessons-in-structure** principle skill).\n\n## Audit the log against the transcript\n\nAt the end of the run, before handing back, check the log told the truth. Read this run's transcript under Claude Code's per-project transcripts directory at `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects/<encoded-cwd>/`, which `node <plugin>/skills/reflect/scripts/claude-project-dir.mjs` prints. Don't glob across `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects/`. That reads unrelated private chats. Walk this run's rows against what actually happened. Each stretch of them begins at one of this run's `start` rows, or at the first row if this run created the log, and ends at the next `start` row of another run:\n\n- Check that every row maps to a real decision or action.\n- Check that each row's evidence resolves and shows what the row claims.\n- A fork, pivot, or abandoned approach that shaped the work but isn't logged is a gap. Add it.\n\nCorrect the log, not the story. The audit never edits or removes a row, even an invented one. When a row records neither a real decision nor a real action, or its claim or evidence is wrong, add a row that supersedes it with what actually happened and a pointer that resolves. This audit does not check rows outside this run's stretches. If this run's own work shows one of them is wrong, supersede it like any wrong call.\n\n## Cross-model review of the trail\n\nBefore handing back, spawn a subagent on a different model family from the one that did the work. Self-review is not a substitute. The subagent reads the audit trail and the run's transcript, then flags what the user should pay attention to. Not a redo of the work, a scan for what's suboptimal or risky. Pick each subagent's reasoning level for the job, per the Subagents section of the **poteto-mode** skill.\n\n- Decisions logged with weak or absent evidence.\n- Verification steps skipped or claimed without proof in the transcript.\n- Choices that look risky in hindsight (premature, scope-creeping, papering over a symptom).\n- Gaps the user would otherwise miss on a casual skim.\n\nEvery reply for a run that produced a trail ends with an \"Attention\" section. Lead with the reviewer's model on its own line (`reviewed by <model>`), then list each flag pointing to specific rows or moments. \"No flags\" is a valid value. The model name is not.\n\n## Reviewing the trail\n\nRead top to bottom, follow the evidence pointers, spot-check. GitHub renders a committed TSV as a table. `column -s$'\\t' -t decisions.tsv` renders it in a terminal.\n\n## Composing this skill\n\nOther skills route their audit trail here instead of inventing one. Reference it by name and let it own the format. Don't restate the columns.\n"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792684472.2022073}}

{"type":"custom_tool_call","id":"ctc_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada31bef48087d080758b3e22663b9e","status":"completed","call_id":"call_ccea7efd7cb64c51a837b0e1b4460a59","name":"exec","input":"text((await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy sed -n '135,175p' /home/kusutohu/.codex/plugins/cache/pstack-claude/pstack/0.9.101/skills/poteto-mode/SKILL.md\",max_output_tokens:5000})).output);\ntext((await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy cat reports/2026-10-22-water-hit-depth/SUMMARY.md\",workdir:\"/home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen\",max_output_tokens:8500})).output);\ntext((await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy cat reports/2026-10-22-water-hit-depth/TRANSCRIPT-DIGEST.md\",workdir:\"/home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen\",max_output_tokens:7000})).output);\n","internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792684478.9585702}}

## 2026-10-22T15:54:42.775Z source line 59704

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16452-60d7-7962-bf59-f81eb0860247","call_id":"call_ccea7efd7cb64c51a837b0e1b4460a59","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.7 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"- **Bug fix.** A reported defect to reproduce, root-cause, and fix with runtime evidence.\n- **Perf issue.** A measured slowness to trace and improve against a baseline.\n- **Hillclimb.** Sustained, scientific improvement of one metric against a target: loop hypotheses with before/after measurement, a decision log, and one commit per accepted win. Distinct from Perf issue, which is a one-off fix.\n- **Runtime forensics.** Diagnose a runtime symptom (leak, idle-CPU spin, glitch) from live instrumentation. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix.\n- **Trace forensics.** Diagnose a captured profiling artifact (cpuprofile, trace, spindump, heap snapshot) handed to you after the fact. The deliverable is a diagnosis, not a fix.\n- **Feature.** New or changed behavior, built from a named data shape.\n- **Refactoring.** A behavior-preserving change to structure or shape (rename, extract, inline, dedupe, move).\n- **Prototype.** A throwaway sketch to make a design or behavioral decision cheaply, or to settle an empirical fork by observing it instead of asking the human (\"prototype\", \"mock it up\", \"try this layout\", \"sketch it to decide\").\n- **Visual parity.** Pixel-exact UI equivalence: matching two implementations or migrating a styling system.\n- **Authoring or modifying a skill.** Writing or editing a SKILL.md. File: `playbooks/authoring-a-skill.md`.\n- **Eval.** Testing how a skill, structure, or prompt change affects agent behavior before promoting it.\n- **Babysit.** Driving a PR or a stack to merge-ready: conflicts, review threads, CI.\n- **Shipping.** The half after Babysit. Independently verifying a green stack, then landing the contiguous verified run bottom-up through `gh` by default or Origin when its CLI is available.\n- **Autonomous run.** A long task to drive to completion without stopping (\"run until done\", \"/loop until X\").\n- **Orchestrate.** A standing project handed to one coordinator chat: multi-day, many stacked PRs, dozens to hundreds of subagents, minimal human turns (\"run this whole project\", \"own this migration until it lands\"). Distinct from Autonomous run, which drives one task to a predicate. Work one agent could finish inside the session's budget routes there, not here, however program-shaped the phrasing sounds.\n- **Autopilot-full.** A queue of independent PRs driven to merge-ready with full autonomy. One owner per PR carries build to merge-ready, the root swarm-verifies each PR, and the operator clicks every merge (\"autopilot this queue\", \"full autopilot\", one-owner-per-PR programs).\n- **Autopilot-stack.** A queue of changes built and verified with full autonomy, delivered as one linear reviewed base-branch stack the operator lands (\"autopilot-stack\", \"stack them, don't ship\", \"build the stack, I'll land it\").\n- **Session pickup.** Resuming or taking over a prior agent's in-flight work from a transcript, cloud-agent URL, or pushed branch.\n- **Pause safely.** Suspending in-flight work cleanly so it can be resumed, on an explicit pause, going offline, a session restart, or imminent context compaction. The complement to Session pickup.\n- **Multi-phase or multi-PR plan.** Work that spans phases or stacked PRs. File: `playbooks/multi-phase-plan.md`.\n- **Worktree and simulator cleanup.** Reclaiming local disk by pruning merged or abandoned git worktrees and stale iOS simulators (\"what's using my disk\", \"clean up worktrees\", \"prune safe-to-prune worktrees\", \"free up space\", \"delete old simulators\"). File: `playbooks/worktree-cleanup.md`.\n- **Opening a PR.** Invoked at the end of every other playbook.\n\n**Project playbooks.** A repository can add playbooks of its own under `.agents/playbooks/`, one Markdown file each. Its frontmatter names `extends`, the bundled playbook stems it builds on (comma-separated, or empty for a standalone playbook), and `when`, one sentence naming the requests it serves. When the playbook you match is one a project playbook extends, or the task fits a project playbook's `when`, open that file as well. Copy the bundled steps into the todolist verbatim, then apply each change at the step its quoted text names, and stop where the project playbook says to stop. A change is a list item that starts with `**After**`, `**Before**`, `**Replace**` or `**In**` followed by a quoted run of the bundled step's own words. Before you apply a project playbook, run `node <plugin>/skills/poteto-mode/scripts/check-playbooks.mjs` from the repository root. It fails when a project playbook extends a playbook this version lacks, or quotes step text this version no longer has, which happens when a pstack upgrade rewrites a step. Report each line it prints to the user and do not guess where an unanchored change belongs.\n\n## Models\n\nRole defaults, stamped from `plugins/pstack/models.json` (edit there, rerun `tools/generate.mjs`). A matching role line in the `pstack-models.md` override sheet overrides each at runtime; `/setup-pstack` writes it and lists its path per runtime.\n\n- feature, refactoring: `sonnet`\n- bug-fix: `opus`\n- perf-issue: `opus`\n- hillclimb: `opus`\n- judgment and prose: `opus`\n- strongest judgment: `fable`\n\n## Reasoning effort\n\nA role value in the override sheet may name a reasoning effort after its model, as in `opus @xhigh`. Levels on Claude Code: `low`, `medium`, `high`, `xhigh`, `max`. Which ones apply depends on the model. A value without `@` takes the sheet's `default effort` line, a level or `session`, and `session` when the sheet has no such line. `session` sets no effort, so the dispatch is the usual one. Strip the suffix before reading the model: `inherit-parent` or `auto` still omits `model` at every level, and a model name is passed as `model`. Pass the level as the dispatch's effort field and keep the `subagent_type` you would otherwise use. That is `effort` on the Claude Code `Agent` call and `reasoning_effort` on Codex's `spawn_agent`. On Muse it is `effort` on a Workflow child; a `subagent_spawn` dispatch keeps the session's effort.\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"# 水面の深度と水中減衰を統一するTEST\n\n連続水面を使う前回のhit1候補に残っていた、光学計算の交点と深度・水中減衰距離の不一致を修正しました。実RTX 3080 Ti、Minecraft 1.21.1／Iris 1.8.8、1600×900の隔離TESTで確認しました。魚眼状の湾曲や折返し全体を解消したという結果ではありません。実画像にも残っています。\n\nCodexが実装・GPU検証・Minecraft操作を担当しました。実Claude CodeのOpusが独立設計、設計の再検討、コードレビューと診断用データの設計を担当しました。Claudeの呼び出しはread-onlyで、Claudeが実装したとは扱っていません。応答、モデル、セッションID、終了コードは各`claude-*`に保存しています。\n\n## 確認した結果\n\n| 記録 | 検査した水面画素 | 減衰距離と光学交点距離の最大差 |\n|---|---:|---:|\n| 修正前・近距離 | 1,440,000 | 約57.68 mm |\n| 修正後・近距離 | 1,440,000 | 約0.00134 mm |\n| 修正前・深い位置、診断値で水面と識別 | 1,439,567 | 約75.96 mm |\n| 修正後・深い位置、診断値で水面と識別 | 1,439,370 | 約0.05022 mm |\n\n修正後は、対象水面の深度・距離・Beer透過率の違反がすべて0です。事前に宣言した距離1 mm、D24深度1量子、透過率絶対差1e-6の基準を変更していません。最遠の対象交点は約5.50 mで、遠距離一般の精度を証明する検査ではありません。\n\n近距離はgeneration2の記録、深い位置はgeneration3の明示的な描画診断付き記録です。generation3は非水面の診断出力だけを追加しています。水面・頂点・composite・物理モデルとcontrol ZIPはgeneration2から変更していません。完了済みの近距離Minecraft撮影は再実行せず、保存データを最終監査コードで再検査しました。\n\n法線、光学交点、視線は比較可能な水面で完全一致しました。深い位置の両記録は1,438,937画素で相互比較できます。C1のラスタ位置、視線C14、opaque depthtex1はフレーム全体でも同一でした。物理モデルや露出を変えて輪郭を隠す変更ではありません。\n\n## 非水面の描画を、水面の失敗として数えていた問題\n\n初期の深い位置では修正前445画素、修正後426画素が厳密検査に失敗しました。どちらも保存しています。誤差は数mに達し、深度精度では説明できません。小さな四角い描画箇所で、水面の記録だけが古く残っていました。\n\nそのため、既知の非水面プログラムが描画した画素には、C12のゼロとC3の(0,0,0,-3)を同時に書く診断を追加しました。距離が合わない画素を条件で除外する方法ではありません。修正前の新記録で433画素、修正後で630画素に正確な診断値を観測しました。残った水面の違反は0です。\n\nこの値は「最後に既知の非水面プログラムが書いた」という意味です。深度の所有者や最終画像の全寄与を意味しません。深度を書かない半透明描画も診断値を残すことをGPUで確認しています。粒子等の時間は固定していないため、過去445画素との座標一致は要求していません。未対応の描画経路は残っています。\n\n## 実装と検証\n\n受理した交点を`gl_FragDepth`に投影し、compositeが同じ水面までの距離を復元します。初期トレース距離と影の評価点も交点に合わせました。非水面や未受理の経路ではラスタ深度に戻ります。太陽光の入射面高さなど、別の近似はこの変更では直していません。\n\n実NVIDIAのネイティブ検査では、旧方式の2,611画素の深度不一致と、遮蔽物を挟む両方向の2,593画素の前後関係誤りが、補正後は0になりました。完全遮蔽、air・unknown-water・nonwaterの一致、8MRTの水面C0／深度一致を確認しました。これはそのGPU検証器の結果であり、Minecraft内の遮蔽物検査ではありません。\n\n非水面の診断はunlit／litの2系統で、深度書込あり・なしの両方を検査しました。各289画素に診断値が現れ、C0と深度は診断なしとバイト一致しました。ブレンドを有効にした誤設定、discard、遮蔽、合成した水面レコードによる再描画も検査しています。最後の再描画はプロトコル用の合成シェーダーであり、実水面プログラムの包括的検査ではありません。\n\n58件のテストが成功しました。保存された実機記録に距離・透過率・深度・交点・opaque同値・誤ったpack hashを個別に注入すると検査が失敗することも確認しました。\n\n途中で検証器の逆射影行列の初期値不足と、Irisのunsized 24-bit深度形式への未対応を発見しました。旧失敗を保存し、新しい検証器では行列積を検査、読み取り側では実形式・ビット数・型を記録しています。RGBA32Fの色検査を緩めた変更ではありません。\n\n## 性能と通常設定\n\nこのTESTは非常に重く、通常プレイ向けではありません。修正後の単発記録は近距離約1.90 FPS、深い位置約2.36 FPSでした。p50は約453／409 msです。1600×900の計測用構成であり、通常0.6や目標2560×1440のFPSとしては扱いません。繰り返し交互計測と律速のプロファイルがないため、高速化・低速化の判定は保留です。生データは`TIMING-OBSERVATIONS.json`です。\n\nLabは通常のAqualumen 0.6／physics 0.2に戻しました。config・Mods・instance・通常ワールドのハッシュが一致し、通常ワールドの巻き戻しはしていません。最終generation3のlauncher終了コードは両方0、Lab Javaなし、TEST操作マーカーなしを確認しています。過去generation2 candidateの外側ラッパー143は別記録に残し、終了コード0とは扱っていません。\n\n通常の`shaders/`と既存の未コミット変更は保持しました。今回の候補は固定17秒・相互作用リップル0・既知の深い水・限定footprint専用で、通常版に導入していません。\n\n## 残る検証\n\n湾曲した輪郭全体の見た目、二次水面・R2、画素内の可視性、メッシュ外の被覆、天井との接触、動く波、一般ワールド、実Minecraftでの遮蔽物との前後関係、影と光源側の放射輸送、通常プレイ性能は未完了です。次の実装単位は交点計算の軽量化と、その後の可視性検査です。このTESTを通常版として出荷する段階ではありません。\n\nModel the Domainでは交点を一つの`WaterPrimaryHit`にまとめました。Fix Root Causesでは見た目を補正する前に、その交点を深度の基準にしました。Boundary DisciplineではIrisの実ストレージを読み取り境界で検査します。Build the Leverでは再実行可能な生成・GPU検査・保存記録監査を残しました。Prove It Worksではコンパイルだけで終わらせず実Irisまで確認しています。\n\n主要な証拠は`CLOSURE.json`、`candidate-near-audit-final.json`、`candidate-deep-owned-audit.json`、`native-depth2`、`native-ownership3`、`final-checks`です。\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"# {"type":"custom_tool_call","id":"ctc_0f4f893e2d74b039016ada31c8481c87d0b925fe02556e20a1","status":"completed","call_id":"call_ef4a15227f3344d2809d5d5355dc3fb9","name":"exec","input":"text((await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy cat /home/kusutohu/.codex/plugins/cache/pstack-claude/pstack/0.9.101/skills/poteto-mode/references/codex-tools.md\",max_output_tokens:9000})).output);\ntext((await tools.exec_command({cmd:\"rtk proxy cat .audit/depth-consistency-2026-10-22.tsv\",workdir:\"/home/kusutohu/repos/aqualumen\",max_output_tokens:10000})).output);\ntext(await tools.write_stdin({session_id:97803,chars:\"\",yield_time_ms:1000,max_output_tokens:3000}));\n","internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792684488.4567482}}

## 2026-10-22T15:54:51.775Z source line 59712

{"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16452-83ff-7b03-93a6-520169d9a2b8","call_id":"call_ef4a15227f3344d2809d5d5355dc3fb9","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.2 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"# Codex tool mapping for pstack\n\npstack skills are written in Claude Code tool language (the `Skill` tool, the `Agent` tool, `AskUserQuestion`, Claude model names). On Codex the skills are the same files; only the tool names resolve differently. Read this when a pstack skill names a Claude tool, a driver or bundled skill, or a Claude model. This file is Codex-specific. Gemini CLI, opencode, Prime Agent, and other runtimes must use their own concrete tools, model names, and configuration paths.\n\n## Tool actions\n\n| pstack / Claude action | Codex equivalent |\n|------------------------|------------------|\n| Read a file | `shell` (`cat`, `head`, `tail`) |\n| Create / edit / delete a file | `apply_patch` |\n| Run a shell command | `shell` |\n| Search file contents / find files | `shell` (`rg`, `grep`, `find`, `ls`) |\n| Fetch a URL | `shell` with `curl` / `wget` |\n| Search the web | `web_search` |\n| Invoke a skill (the `Skill` tool, `/command`) | Skills load natively. Follow the instructions presented. |\n| Dispatch a subagent (the `Agent`/`Task` tool) | `spawn_agent` |\n| Dispatch N parallel subagents in one turn | N `spawn_agent` calls in one response |\n| Wait for a subagent result | `wait_agent` |\n| Free a finished subagent slot | `close_agent`, when the session exposes it. Some Codex hosts don't. |\n| Track tasks (the todolist; `TaskCreate` / `TaskUpdate`, or `TodoWrite` on Claude Code) | `update_plan` |\n| Ask the human a fixed-choice question (`AskUserQuestion`) | Ask in plain text and let the user answer. Codex has no structured-choice tool. |\n\nSubagent dispatch needs `multi_agent` enabled. Add to `~/.codex/config.toml`:\n\n```toml\n[features]\nmulti_agent = true\n```\n\nWithout it, `spawn_agent` is unavailable and the fan-out skills (`interrogate`, `why`, `how`, `arena`, `reflect`) degrade to a single sequential pass. A required independent review does not degrade. It stays blocked, as poteto-mode's [Subagents](../SKILL.md#subagents) section says.\n\nEnabling `multi_agent` does not lift the host's thread limit. A thread-limit error from `spawn_agent` is a capacity error, not a rejected model slug, so the skills' fallback to another model does not apply. After the first capacity rejection, stop spawning. Close a finished agent with `close_agent` before you try again, and where the session has no `close_agent`, do not assume a finished agent's slot is free.\n\n## Subagent policy\n\npoteto-mode's Subagents section sets Claude-specific defaults (`subagent_type: \"pstack:poteto-agent\"`, `run_in_background: true`). On Codex:\n\n- There is no `poteto-agent` subagent type. Route an ad-hoc subagent through poteto-mode's style by dispatching a `spawn_agent` whose instructions tell it to read the `poteto-mode` skill in full first.\n- `spawn_agent` calls already run concurrently with your turn, so `run_in_background: true` has no separate flag. Issue the dispatch and continue.\n- When a role value carries `@<level>`, or the `default effort` line names a level, pass that level as `spawn_agent`'s `reasoning_effort` and keep the dispatch otherwise unchanged. `session` passes no `reasoning_effort`.\n- There is no `comment-sicko` subagent type either. The **no-comments** skill spawns it on Claude Code; on Codex dispatch a `spawn_agent` whose instructions tell it to read `poteto-mode/references/agents/comment-sicko.md` in full first.\n- There are no `thermo-nuclear-review-subagent` or `thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review-subagent` types. The **thermos** skill spawns both on Claude Code; on Codex dispatch two `spawn_agent` calls whose instructions tell each to read the `thermo-nuclear-review` or `thermo-nuclear-code-quality-review` skill in full first and review only the diff and file contents in its prompt.\n- Claude Code runs every subagent on this machine, so the **swarm** skill's workers and the fan-out playbooks (`orchestrate`, `autopilot-full`, `autopilot-stack`) isolate writers with worktrees. The same holds on Codex.\n- Keep the rest of the policy unchanged. Pass file pointers not inlined context, give each worker its own worktree or branch when they write, review every subagent's diff yourself.\n\n## Model names\n\nSkills name Claude defaults (a single-role default for code/prose/judgment, a worker model for feature and refactoring code, plus a diverse-model panel for diverse-model panels; each model-consuming skill lists its own in a Models section). These slugs do not resolve on Codex. Substitute your configured Codex models:\n\n- Single-model roles: your primary Codex model (for example `gpt-6-sol`).\n- Roles that default to the Claude worker model (`feature, refactoring`): your fast Codex code model (for example `gpt-6-sol`).\n- Roles that default to the strongest Claude model (`strongest judgment`): your strongest Codex model (for example `gpt-6-astra`).\n- Diverse-model panels (`arena`, `architect`, `interrogate`, `how` critics, `reflect`): the adversarial signal comes from model diversity, so use the distinct Codex models available to you. A good default panel on ChatGPT is `gpt-6-astra`, `gpt-6-sol`, `gpt-6-luna`. If only one model family is reachable, vary reasoning effort and note in the verdict that diversity was reduced.\n\n`/setup-pstack` writes the configured model list. On Codex, set it to your Codex model slugs.\n\n## Session routing hook\n\nThe native pstack plugin bundles the same `SessionStart` routing instruction as the Claude Code plugin, through `session-start.sh` on macOS and Linux and `session-start.ps1` on Windows. Codex runs the hook on startup, resume, clear, and compact after the user trusts the hook through `/hooks`. The hook reads `session hook` from the Codex sheet, at the path in [setup-pstack's runtime table](../../setup-pstack/SKILL.md#other-runtimes); `session hook: off` disables injection.\n\nA skills-only installation does not include plugin hooks. Request `poteto-mode` explicitly or add a standing instruction to `AGENTS.md` in that case.\n\n## Driver and bundled skills pstack references\n\nThe [driver policy](../SKILL.md#non-negotiables) selects the app driver. For skills and drivers named by these workflows, use these Codex equivalents:\n\n| Skill or driver named in pstack | On Codex |\n|---------------------------------|----------|\n| `run` (drive a CLI/TUI to see a change work) | Run the app yourself via `shell` and observe the real output. |\n| Project UI driver | Drive the UI with whatever automation you have, or hand the user a concrete manual check. Do not claim done without observing the artifact. |\n| `plugin-dev:skill-development` (Claude's SKILL.md authoring guidance) | Follow your platform's skill-authoring guidance; the `writing-skills` skill if present. Keep `name` + `description` frontmatter and progressive disclosure. |\n| `loop` (recurring/self-paced re-invocation, used by `babysit`) | Codex has no `loop` skill. Re-run the step yourself on a cadence, or use a Codex scheduled task if available. |\n\n## Per-skill notes\n\nAffected skill entry points and the optional Codex slash stubs point here. Most skills need only the tables above. These need one more mapping:\n\n| Skill | On Codex |\n|-------|----------|\n| `interrogate` | The `subagent_type`/`model`/`readonly` dispatch fields map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models and keep the reviewer panel model-diverse. |\n| `setup-pstack` | The skill's Other runtimes table names the Codex sheet path and how it loads; the slugs are your Codex models (see Model names above). The role rows are identical. |\n| `no-comments` | There is no `comment-sicko` subagent type; see Subagent policy above. |\n| `thermos` | There are no thermo-nuclear subagent types; see Subagent policy above. |\n| `teach` | Running `how` and `why` in parallel maps to `spawn_agent` fan-out; image generation uses the configured Codex equivalent. |\n| `create-verification-skill` | The generated skill lands under `.claude/skills/verify/` on Claude Code; write it to Codex's project-skill location instead. The app-driving harness is platform-neutral. |\n| `maintain-verification-skill` | The parallel per-feature source readers map to `spawn_agent` fan-out; the project-local skill lives under Codex's skills location, not `.claude/skills/`. |\n| `create-formal-verification-skill` | Write the generated skill to Codex's project-skill location, named for the area it verifies. The verifier and its run command are platform-neutral. |\n| `maintain-formal-verification-skill` | Project skills, the formal verification skill among them, live under Codex's project-skill location. |\n| `babysit` | `loop` and `AskUserQuestion` resolve through the tables above. |\n| `automate-me` | `plugin-dev:skill-development` resolves through the skills table above. |\n| `architect` | The runner panel goes through the **arena** skill, so its `spawn_agent` fan-out and model substitution apply here too. |\n| `arena` | The parallel candidates and the cross-judge map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models for the runners and the cross-judge pool (see Model names above). |\n| `how` | The parallel explorers and the explainer map to `spawn_agent` fan-out; substitute your configured Codex models. |\n| `reflect` | The three reviewers and the synthesizer map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models. The transcript finder reads Claude Code's layout under `${CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR:-$HOME/.claude}/projects/`, so pass the session digest step 1 allows instead. |\n| `swarm` | Each worker is a `spawn_agent` call on your configured Codex model, and those calls already run concurrently; give each writing worker its own worktree or output directory (see Subagent policy above). |\n| `why` | The parallel investigators and the synthesizer map to `spawn_agent`; substitute your configured Codex models. List MCP servers from the tools Codex exposes to the session, not from `.mcp.json` or `claude mcp list`. |\n\n## Vendored scripts\n\n`skills/poteto-mode/scripts/` ships the `watch-pr` PR watcher, the `orch` store CLI, and `worktree-audit.mjs`. The `watch-pr/ship-pr` command owns pending-merge inspection and cancellation; `resume.mjs` owns the shared checkpoint locator described in [Resume storage](resume-storage.md). These scripts use bun and Node.js and run the same on Codex; invoke them through `shell`. They need `bun` and `gh`, or `origin` when the forge is Origin. Run `worktree-audit.mjs` with `node`, as its shebang does. The audit follows each link in a worktree's ancestor directories with `stat` and matches it by the directory it lands on, under bun and node alike. A worktree whose own path bun cannot resolve loses its last chat and lands in `review`. A dangling or looping link is ignored. Any other link that `stat` cannot follow puts every worktree under the link's directory in `review`, with a warning that names the link. `worktree-audit.mjs` scans Codex sessions under `$CODEX_HOME/sessions` and `$CODEX_HOME/archived_sessions` (default `~/.codex`), along with any Claude Code or Pi transcript directory that exists. It imports the transcript walker from `skills/reflect/scripts/find-transcript.mjs`, so keep the `reflect` skill installed beside `poteto-mode`.\n\n## Instructions file\n\nWhere a pstack skill says \"your instructions file\", on Codex that is `AGENTS.md` (project root, plus `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` global). On Claude Code it is `CLAUDE.md`.\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"ts\tphase\tdecision\twhy\tevidence\tresult\n2026-10-22T14:32:11Z\tstart\tContinue from accepted hit1 optics into real depth and attenuation consistency\tUser authorized actual Claude and Codex collaboration; {"type":"custom_tool_call_output","id":"ctco_01a16452-9ef9-7590-a497-f23a9dc24c04","call_id":"call_5c07c6434b534c89b69346526f8f550e","output":[{"type":"input_text","text":"Script completed\nWall time 0.2 seconds\nOutput:\n"},{"type":"input_text","text":"I made about 14 read-only file reads within the allowed budget. I ran no commands. Nothing here is runtime verification.\n\n**No blocking findings.** I found nothing new that shows the generation3 witness is invalid or circular, and I'm not reopening the rejected join with the old 445 coordinates.\n\n## What I checked\n\n- **The marker is independent of the error being measured.** The marker comes only from patched nonwater fragment shaders (`tools/water_hit_depth_ownership.py:129-137`). It is not chosen by looking at depth or distance error. Every pixel classified as water still uses the original budgets: 1 mm distance, one D24 depth step and 1e-6 Beer (`tools/water_hit_depth_audit.py:89-103`). A nonwater program without the marker that wrote depth over water would leave a stale water record. That pixel would then fail, not be hidden. The marker checks are strict: exact all-zero C12, exact zero C3 xyz, and no overlap with water (`water_hit_depth_audit.py:110-112`).\n- **Water's own blend state did not change in generation3.** This was my main suspicion. The base properties set blending for the whole `gbuffers_water` program (`shaders/shaders.properties:17`), and the new key list includes `gbuffers_water`/`hand_water`. However:\n  - The generator only appends keys that are missing, and it raises an error if an existing key is not `off` (`water_hit_depth_ownership.py:141-145`).\n  - A test shows that appending `blend.gbuffers_water.colortex12=off` to the generation2 pack raises a duplicate-key error. So the key is already there (`tools/test_water_hit_depth_ownership.py:82-86`), and all existing keys keep their values (`:67-68`).\n  - The optical probe already sets water C3 blending to off (`tools/water_optical_probe.py:16-19,94`).\n  - Only the two patched shader files and the properties file can change (`water_hit_depth_ownership.py:176-178`).\n- **The native test covers the opaque terrain path too.** `gbuffers_terrain.fsh` and `gbuffers_textured_lit.fsh` have the same pinned hash (`CLOSURE.json:33,37`). So the textured_lit native test compiles the same bytes that opaque terrain uses.\n- **The saved numbers agree with each other.**\n  - 1439370 + 630 = 1440000, and 1439567 + 433 = 1440000.\n  - `visibleWaterChangedPixels` = 1063 = 433 + 630. This means the baseline3 and candidate3 marker sets share no pixels at all. That fits live particles, and it further supports rejecting a same-pixel join with old frames.\n  - Mutual pixels: 1440000 − 1063 = 1438937.\n  - The maximum candidate deep error is 5.02e-5 m, and the farthest accepted point is 5.50 m. Both match `SUMMARY.md:14,16`.\n- **The native overlay test is meaningful.** It requires more than 100 markers and zero markers in the old program, the same C0 and depth with and without the diagnostic, and depth changes that match the depth mask (`tools/water_hit_depth_ownership_native.cpp:86-91`). It also requires the blend-on, discard and occlusion controls to behave as expected (`:97-102`).\n\n## Minor, non-blocking issues\n\n1. **Low: the generation3 audit doesn't enforce two things it reports.** `declaredCheckPassed` does not require `nonwaterWithoutExactMarkerPixels == 0` or a nonzero marker count (`water_hit_depth_audit.py:102-118`). The \"zero unmarked nonwater\" statement is backed by the saved value (`candidate-deep-owned-audit.json:114`), not by a pass condition. This doesn't weaken the water claim, because unmarked nonwater pixels are never part of the water check. But a future run with a gap in markers would still pass. Suggestion: describe it as observed, or add it as a pass condition before any rerun.\n2. **Informational: `stale_old` is printed, not counted.** The overlay output prints `stale_old=<count>` (`water_hit_depth_ownership_native.cpp:95`). The real check is that every pixel stayed water in the old program (`:89`), which is stronger. The printed number just repeats the marker count. The wording is cosmetic, and the SUMMARY doesn't rely on that field.\n3. **Informational: one manifest field is hard-coded.** `waterVertexCompositeBytesExact: True` is a literal (`water_hit_depth_ownership.py:203`). The fact itself is enforced by the changed-file check at `:176-178`, so the field is accurate, but it isn't measured on its own.\n4. **Informational: naming.** `historicalGeneration2CandidateWrapperExitCodeObserved: false` (`CLOSURE.json:156`) is consistent with keeping the wrapper's 143 on record and not calling it exit 0. A reader could still take \"observed\" to mean the 143 was never seen. A short pointer to `LAUNCHER-EXIT-ADDENDUM.md` would remove that ambiguity.\n\n## Accepted limitations (correctly left open, no action needed)\n\n- The marker means \"last known nonwater diagnostic writer\". It does not mean depth or beauty ownership. Depth-mask-false overlays legitimately mark pixels without changing depth.\n- Blending is configured off for all 27 program IDs, but Iris's fallback source resolution was not observed. The exact −3 values in the actual captures only show that blending was off for the programs that actually drew here.\n- The native fixture sets blend state directly, not by parsing Iris properties. The reclaim control is a synthetic shader, not the real water program.\n- Not covered: `gbuffers_basic`, `skybasic`, `line`, and the nonwater branch of the water program; arbitrary packs and dimensions; and depth-stencil sampling with its fallbacks.\n- No actual matrices were captured. The eye-relative origin is checked only through the baseline raster chain, to 1 mm. Actual water draw state, actual opaque straddles and ceilings were not observed. There is no proof that the C0 of the instrumented and uninstrumented whole scenes match.\n- The scope is the frozen 17 s scene, zero ripples, known deep water and a points up to about 5.5 m away. No claim about general distances or worlds.\n- The timings (1.90/2.36 FPS) are single instrumented runs. There is no profiling or interleaved repeat, so there is no speedup or regression verdict and no production FPS number.\n- Full visual, physical, R2, dynamic, general-world, performance and release gates remain open.\n\n`CLOSURE.json` and `SUMMARY.md` state these accurately. This finishes only this bounded unit (hit, depth and eye attenuation plus the known-nonwater writer protocol). Requires EGL and libepoxy development files.\n- Record the GL renderer so software results cannot be mistaken for NVIDIA results. Follow current tool permissions for GPU access.\n- `physics-mod/` contains the read-only client contact bridge, pinned to Iris 1.8.8 / Minecraft 1.21.1. Build via `tools/build-physics-mod.sh`.\n- Persistent interaction waves use `setup/begin/begin1.csh`; check CFL, image barriers and camera-relative coordinates when editing.\n- Benchmark automation is opt-in and exact-world UUID guarded; never enable it for ordinary user worlds.\n- Preserve existing Minecraft instances, configs and worlds. Only install to the separate `Aqualumen Lab` instance.\n- Distinguish analytical/approximate rendering, actual fluid simulation, GPU harness timings and Minecraft frame timings in reports.\n- No purchased assets or copied shader-pack sources. Cite algorithm references in README.\n"}],"internal_chat_message_metadata_passthrough":{"turn_id":"01a16402-ca14-7ff1-b506-ddb21644f9b6","create_time":1792684517.6811907}}
